(Indra Speaks),— It is not certain what to-day or what tomorrow will yield to us : who comprehends this mystery ? Verily; the mind of any other (being) is of an unsteady (nature), and even that which has been profoundly studied, is (in time) forgotten.

### Printed p. 178

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

न नूनं (*na nūnaṃ*)—अद्यतनं यदस्ति तत् अद्य नास्ति । नूनमत्र विचिकित्सार्थे । (*adyatanaṃ yadasti tat adya nāsti | nūnamatra vicikitsārthe |*) Among the three kinds of time-made distinctions, past, present and future, the "today" distinction, i.e. that of the present, what is now going on, does not exist: this is the meaning. In this Ṛk the word अद्य (*adya*) is not used; yet since in the following sentence the word श्वः (*śvaḥ*) (tomorrow), denoting future time, is used, it is proper to supply अद्य (*adya*) here; so Sāyaṇa explains: अद्य इति यद्यपि नास्ति तथापि उत्तरवाक्ये श्वः इति दर्शनात् अद्येति गम्यते (*adya iti yadyapi nāsti tathāpi uttaravākye śvaḥ iti darśanāt adyeti gamyate*). Yāska: नूनमिति विचिकित्सार्थीयो भाषायामुभयमन्वध्यायं विचिकित्सार्थीयश्च पदपूरणश्च (*nūnamiti vicikitsārthīyo bhāṣāyāmubhayamanvadhyāyaṃ vicikitsārthīyaśca padapūraṇaśca*) (Ni. 1-5). The word नूनं (*nūnaṃ*) is used in the sense of doubt elsewhere too, not only in the Vedic metres; in the metres it is used both in the sense of doubt and as a filler. Durgācārya explains: विचिकित्सा नाम विवेकपूर्वको अवधारणाभिप्रायः (*vicikitsā nāma vivekapūrvako avadhāraṇābhiprāyaḥ*)—in situations where doubt has arisen, determination by examination is what the word विचिकित्सा (*vicikitsā*) conveys. Sāyaṇa upholds that नूनं (*nūnaṃ*) is used here in the sense of विचिकित्सा (*vicikitsā*).

श्वः नूनं न (*śvaḥ nūnaṃ na*)—तथा श्वस्तनमपि नैव नास्ति (*tathā śvastanamapi naiva nāsti*): as there is no present-time distinction, so also there is no future-time designation "tomorrow": this is the meaning. Here Sāyaṇa cites Yāska: अत्र यास्कः—अगस्त्य इंद्राय हविर्निरुप्य मरुद्भ्यः संप्रदित्सांचकार स इंद्र एत्य परिदेवयांचक्रे (*atra yāskaḥ—agastya iṃdrāya havirnirupya marudbhyaḥ saṃpraditsāṃcakāra sa iṃdra etya paridevayāṃcakre*) (Ni. 1-5). Yāska's meaning: when the sage Agastya, having prepared an oblation for Indra, wished to offer it to the Maruts, Indra, because the oblation due to him was being offered otherwise, expressed the grief of his mind by this mantra; and Sāyaṇa quotes that statement.

अद्य यथा अस्मदर्थं निरुप्तं अस्मभ्यं नास्तिकिलश्वः कथं लप्स्यते (*adya yathā asmadarthaṃ niruptaṃ asmabhyaṃ nāstikilaśvaḥ kathaṃ lapsyate*): when the oblation prepared for me today does not reach me, how can I be sure of obtaining it tomorrow? Thus the manner of Indra's grief is described.

यदद्भुतं तत् को वेद (*yadadbhutaṃ tat ko veda*)—अन्यस्मै निरुप्य अन्यस्मै दत्तमिति यदस्ति वैपरीत्यं तत् को वेद (*anyasmai nirupya anyasmai dattamiti yadasti vaiparītyaṃ tat ko veda*). One kind of meaning is set out: no one knows that there will be such a reversal as an oblation prepared for one being offered to another.

### Printed p. 179
यद्वा यदद्भुतं भावि कार्यं यदस्ति तत्को वेद अद्य श्वो वा लभ्यते इत्याशा न कार्या (*yadvā yadadbhutaṃ bhāvi kāryaṃ yadasti tatko veda adya śvo vā labhyate ityāśā na kāryā*). Since no one knows what wonderful event will occur in future, hope that the prepared oblation will be obtained today or tomorrow cannot be entertained: a statement expressing Indra's grief. This second meaning too is explained.

अभि संचरेण्यं चित्तं (*abhi saṃcareṇyaṃ cittaṃ*)—सर्वतः संचारि अन्यस्य मानसं (*sarvataḥ saṃcāri anyasya mānasaṃ*): the mind of others is always of a roving nature and not steady. उत अपिच आधीतं विनश्यति (*uta apica ādhītaṃ vinaśyati*)—even though I, Indra, was sure that the oblation prepared for me would reach me, it is lost, it does not reach me; and who knows the non-attainment of this wonderful oblation to come? Thus Indra's distress is described.

Yāska quotes and explains this Ṛk; in it he shows that नूनं (*nūnaṃ*) is used in the sense of doubt (determination): न नूनमस्त्यद्यतनं नो एव श्वस्तनं । अद्य आस्मिन् द्यवि द्युरित्यहो नामधेयं द्योतत इति सतः । श्व उपाशंसनीयः कालः । ह्यो हीनः कालः । कस्तद्वेदयदद्भुतं कस्तद्वेद यदभूत् । इदमपीतरदद्भुतमभूतमिव । अन्यस्य चित्तमभिसंचरेण्यं अभिसंचारि ॥ अन्नो नानेयः । चित्तं जीततेः । उताधीतं विनश्यतीति । अप्याध्यातं विनश्यतीति । आध्यातं अभिप्रेतं (*na nūnamastyadyatanaṃ no eva śvastanaṃ | adya āsmin dyavi dyurityaho nāmadheyaṃ dyotata iti sataḥ | śva upāśaṃsanīyaḥ kālaḥ | hyo hīnaḥ kālaḥ | kastadvedayadadbhutaṃ kastadveda yadabhūt | idamapītaradadbhutamabhūtamiva | anyasya cittamabhisaṃcareṇyaṃ abhisaṃcāri ‖ anno nāneyaḥ | cittaṃ jītateḥ | utādhītaṃ vinaśyatīti | apyādhyātaṃ vinaśyatīti | ādhyātaṃ abhipretaṃ*) (Ni. 1-7). The word नूनं (*nūnaṃ*) conveys determination after careful examination. The word द्यवि (*dyavi*), formed from the root द्युत् (*dyut*) meaning shining, denotes the day and is indicated here by अद्यतन (*adyatana*). What was sure to be obtained this day is not obtained; this being so, there is no certainty even in the time hoped for later that the oblation will be obtained. The word श्वः (*śvaḥ*) denotes the time in which hope may be fulfilled, ह्यः (*hyaḥ*) the time gone by. अद्भुत (*adbhuta*) is explained by अभूत् (*abhūt*), the past sense; since what was prepared now for me is not mine, who can be sure it will come later to me or to another? अन्यस्य चित्तं (*anyasya cittaṃ*), the mind of others, अभिसंचरेण्यं (*abhisaṃcareṇyaṃ*)—

### Printed p. 180
roving, of fickle nature; hence what is now prepared does not reach me: this is the meaning. And उत (*uta*), अपि (*api*), अथ (*atha*) are said to be synonyms in the Veda conveying one meaning. For अद्य (*adya*), श्वः (*śvaḥ*), ह्यः (*hyaḥ*), अद्भुतं (*adbhutaṃ*), अन्यः (*anyaḥ*), चित्तं (*cittaṃ*), आधीतं (*ādhītaṃ*) the explanations are: द्योतत इति सतः (*dyotata iti sataḥ*), श्वः उपाशंसनीयः कालः (*śvaḥ upāśaṃsanīyaḥ kālaḥ*), ह्यः हीनः कालः (*hyaḥ hīnaḥ kālaḥ*), अद्भुतं अभूत् (*adbhutaṃ abhūt*), अन्यः नानेयः (*anyaḥ nāneyaḥ*), चित्तं चेततेः (*cittaṃ cetateḥ*), आध्यातं अभिप्रेतं (*ādhyātaṃ abhipretaṃ*).

##### Grammar

- अस्ति—निपातैर्य्यद्यदि- (पा. सू. ८-१-३०) इति निघातप्रतिषेधात् धातुस्वरः अवशिष्यते ।
- वेद—विद ज्ञाने धातुः । विदो लटो वा (पा.सू.३-४-८३) इति लटः तिपः णलादेशः । धातोः लघोपधस्य गुणः । तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति ।
- अद्भुतं—भू सत्तायां धातुः । अस्मिन् अद् इत्युपपदे अदिभुवो डुतच् (उ.सू. ६७९) इति डुतच् प्रत्ययः । डित्त्वात् टेः (ऊ) लोपः । व्यत्ययेन आद्युदात्तस्वरः भवति । **NOTE**
- संचरेण्यं—चर गतिभक्षणयोः धातुः । सम् उपसर्गः । बहुलग्रहणात् औणादिकः एण्यप्रत्ययः । प्रत्ययस्य आद्युदात्तस्वरः भवति । प्रादिसमासे कृदुत्तरपदप्रकृतिस्वरः भवति ।
- आधीतं—इज् अध्ययने धातुः । कर्मणि क्तप्रत्ययः । गतिरनंतरः (पा. सू. ६-२-४९) इति गतेः प्रकृतिस्वरः भवति ।
- नश्यति—णश अदर्शने धातुः । लट् प्रथमपुरुषैकवचनांतरूपं । तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति ॥ १ ॥

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> किं न॑ इंद्र जिघांससि॒ भ्रात॑रो म॒रुत॒स्तव॑ ।  
> तेभिः॑ कल्पस्व साधु॒या मा नः॑ स॒मर॑णे वधीः ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 181

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> किं । नः । इंद्र । जिघांससि । भ्रातरः । मरुतः । शव ।  
> तेभिः । कल्पस्व । साधुया । मा । नः । संअरणे । वधीः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इदमगस्त्यवाक्यं । हे इंद्र नोऽस्थाननपराधिनः किं जिघांससि । हंतुमिच्छसि ।
> कथमवध्यत्वमिति अत आह । मरुतस्तव भ्रातरः । त्वया भोगप्रदानेन भरणीया
> इत्यर्थः । अतो वयमवध्या इत्युक्तं भवति । मरुतामिंद्रभ्रातृत्वमेकस्मिन्नेवादितिगर्भ उत्पन्नत्वात् । सा चोत्पत्तिः पुराणेषु प्रसिद्दा । संतु भ्रातरस्ततश्च किमिति आत
> आह । तेभिस्तैर्मरुद्धिः सह साधुया साधु त्वं कल्पस्व । कल्पय हविर्भागं तैः सह
> युद्धं वा । नोऽस्मान् समरणे संग्रामे मा वधीः । मा हिंसीः । यद्वा ।
> लुप्तो दृष्टांतः । संग्रामे यथा विरोधिनं हंसि न तथास्मानिति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is Agastya's speech. "O Indra, why do you wish to kill us, who are without offence?" How are we not to be killed? To this he says: the Maruts are your brothers, that is, they are to be maintained by you through your granting of enjoyment; hence we are not to be killed. The Maruts' brotherhood with Indra rests on their having been born in the one womb of Aditi, and that origin is well known in the Purāṇas. "Let them be brothers; what then?" To this he says: "with those Maruts, act kindly" — arrange the share of the oblation with them, or else war with them. "Do not kill us in the samaraṇa, the battle." Or else, a simile is omitted: as you strike an enemy in battle, so do not strike us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (हे (*he*)) इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—the Marut deities
- तव भ्रातरः (*tava bhrātaraḥ*)—your brothers (those to be nourished by you)
- नः (*naḥ*)—us (who are without offence)
- किं (*kiṃ*)—why
- जिघांससि (*jighāṃsasi*)—do you wish to kill?
- तेभिः (*tebhiḥ*)—with those Marut deities
- साधुया (*sādhuyā*)—in a peaceful manner
- कल्पय (*kalpaya*)—arrange (share the share of the oblation)
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- सं अरणे (*saṃ araṇe*)—in battle (in war)
- मा वधीः (*mā vadhīḥ*)—do not injure (do not kill)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(Agastya speaks to Indra.) O Indra, the Maruts are under your nourishing care (your brothers). Why do you wish to kill us, who are without offence? Peacefully share the oblation with them; do not kill us in battle (in enmity).

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
(Agastya),— Why, Indra, do you purpose to slay us : the Maruts are your brethren : share with them (the offering) in peace ; destroy us not in enmity.

### Printed p. 182

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this sūkta, which takes the form of a dialogue between Indra and the sage Agastya, the first mantra states that Indra, having understood that Agastya intends to offer to the Maruts the oblation prepared for himself, expressed his regret. That Agastya gave Indra an answer is evident from this mantra. This is what Sāyaṇa describes in the bhāṣya as इदमगस्त्यवाक्यं (*idamagastyavākyaṃ*).
Addressing Indra, नः किं जिघांससि (*naḥ kiṃ jighāṃsasi*)—अस्मान् अनपराधिनः हंतुमिच्छसि किं (*asmān anaparādhinaḥ haṃtumicchasi kiṃ*): by way of a question the sage gives the answer that we, who have done no wrong and are not guilty, should not be subjected to punishment; and to show the reason why we sages do not deserve punishment, he also says the sentence मरुतः तव भ्रातरः (*marutaḥ tava bhrātaraḥ*). त्वया भोगप्रदानेन भरणीयाः (*tvayā bhogapradānena bharaṇīyāḥ*): since the Maruts are nourished by you through your producing enjoyment, we, who wish to offer the oblation to your dependents, are not to be killed; this is the meaning described.
Yāska explains the word bhrātṛ, formed from the root bhṛ, as one who obtains a share or one fit to be maintained: भ्राता भरतेर्हरतिकर्मणो हरते भागं भर्तव्यो भवतीति वा (*bhrātā bharaterharatikarmaṇo harate bhāgaṃ bhartavyo bhavatīti vā*) (Ni. 4-26). Sāyaṇa explains that the Maruts are Indra's brothers because they were born in the very womb of Indra's mother Aditi (एकस्मिन्नेवादितेर्गर्भ उत्पन्नत्वात् (*ekasminnevāditergarbha utpannatvāt*)), and that सा च उत्पत्तिः पुराणादिशु प्रसिद्धा (*sā ca utpattiḥ purāṇādiśu prasiddhā*)—the manner of the Maruts' birth is well known in the Purāṇas and other works. This matter is also stated in the Bṛhaddevatā.
तेभिः साधुया कल्पस्व (*tebhiḥ sādhuyā kalpasva*)—तैर्मरुद्भिस्सह साधुत्वं कल्पय हविर्भागं तैसह युद्धं वा (*tairmarudbhissaha sādhutvaṃ kalpaya havirbhāgaṃ taisaha yuddhaṃ vā*): Agastya, addressing Indra, says: adopt a kindly attitude with your brothers, and either grant them a share of the oblation or else fight with them.
नः समरणे मा वधीः (*naḥ samaraṇe mā vadhīḥ*)—संग्रामे यथा विरोधिनं हंसि (*saṃgrāme yathā virodhinaṃ haṃsi*): do not injure us as you kill an enemy in battle; this answer too is given. One interpretation takes the word of comparison in this sentence to be omitted; another meaning is given: do not wage a war that has no connection with a simile and kill us. The word samaraṇa is stated among the names of battle (Ni. 3-9), and as सह मरणं यस्मिन् (*saha maraṇaṃ yasmin*) it is described as war, the situation in which death comes together to many creatures.

### Printed p. 183

##### Grammar

- जिघांससि — हन हिंसागत्योः धातुः । धातोः कर्मणः समा—(पा. सू. ३-१-७) इति इच्छार्थे सन् । अज्जनगमां सनि (पा. सू. ६-४-१६) इति धातोः उपधायाः दीर्घः । सन्यजोः इति द्वित्वम् । अभ्यासाच्च (पा. सू. ७-३-५५) इति धातोः हकारस्य कुत्वम् । सन्यतः इति सूत्रेण अभ्यासस्य इत्वम् । अतिजंतस्य परत्वात् निघातस्वरः भवति ।
- भ्रातरः — भ्राज्य दीप्तौ धातुः । नप्तृनेष्ट्रु—(उ.सू. २५२) इति तृन्नंततया निपातितः ।
- तेभिः — बहुलं छंदसि इति ऐस्‌भावस्य विकल्पितत्वात् एत्वम् ।
- कल्पस्व — कृपू सामर्थ्य्ये धातुः । लोट् मध्यमपुरुष एकवचनांतरूपम् । थासः से इति प्रत्ययस्य से आदेशे सवाभ्यांवाम् इति टेः वत्वम् । शप् निमित्ततया धातोः लघूपधायाः गुणः । रपरत्वे कृपो रोलः (पा. सू. ८-२-१८) इति लत्वम् । तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति ।
- साधुया — साधुशब्दस्य तृतीयाएकवचने परे सुपांसुलुक् (पा. सू- ७-१-३९) इति तस्य याचादेशः । चितः(पा.सू. ६-१-१६३) इति अंतोदात्तस्वरः भवति ।
- वधीः — हन हिंसागत्योः धातुः । माजिलुज् (पा. सू ३-३-१७५) इति माज् उपपदे लुज् । मध्यमपुरुष एकवचने सिप् आगते इकारलोपः । हनोवधलिज् इति अनुवृत्ते लुजिचॆ (पा. सू. २-४-४३) इति धातोः वधादेशः । च्चेः सिचादेशः । इट ईटि (पा.सू. ८-२-२८) इति तस्य लोपः । न माण्योगे (पा. सू. ६-४-७४) इति अडागमः न आगच्छति । तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति ।

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> किं नो॑ भ्रातरगस्य॒ सखा॒ सन्नति॑ मन्यसे ।  
> वि॒द्या हि ते॒ यथा॒ मनो॒ऽस्मभ्य॒मिन्न दि॑त्ससि ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 184

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> किं । नः । भ्रातः । अगस्त्य । सखा । सन् । अति । मन्यसे ।  
> विद्यॆ । हि । ते । यथा । मनः । अस्मभ्यं । इत् । न । दित्ससि ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इदमिंद्रवाक्यं । हे भ्रातः हे अगस्त्य । हविष्टदानवरप्रदानाभ्यां
> परस्परोपकारत्वाद्भातृत्वं । तादृशस्त्वं सखा सन्नस्मद्धितकार्यपि सन्नोऽस्मान्किमति
> मन्यसे । कस्मादपराधादतिक्रम्य मनुषे । अपलपसि । नायमपलापः किंतु तथ्यमेवेति चेत् उच्यते । गंतव्यं ते तव मनो मानसं यथा यथावस्तु विद्म ।
> जानीमः । कथं जानीथेति उच्यते । अस्मभ्यमिन्न दित्ससि । अस्मदर्थं निरुप्तमस्मभ्यं
> दातुं नेच्छस्येव । इदेवार्थे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is Indra's speech. "O brother, O Agastya": brotherhood arises from mutual benefit, by the gift of the oblation and the granting of a boon. Though you, such a one, are a friend and one who acts for our good, why do you disregard us? Out of what offence do you pass over and "think" (manuṣe)? You deny. If it is said, "this is not a denial but the truth," it is replied: we know your mind as it is. How? "You do not wish to give to us": what was set apart for us you do not wish to give to us at all. The word इत् (*it*) is in the sense of *eva*.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हे भ्रातः अगस्त्य (*he bhrātaḥ agastya*)—O brother Agastya
- सखा सन् (*sakhā san*)—though a friend to me (though a benefactor)
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- किं अति मन्यसे (*kiṃ ati manyase*)—why do you disregard?
- ते (*te*)—your
- मनः (*manaḥ*)—mind
- यथा (*yathā*)—truly
- विद्म हि (*vidma hi*)—we have indeed known
- अस्मभ्यं (*asmabhyaṃ*)—to us
- न दित्ससि इत् (*na ditsasi it*)—you do not wish to give (oblations and the rest) at all

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O brother (Agastya), though you are my friend (a benefactor), why do you treat me with disregard? We know well that you have no wish to give us the oblations and the rest.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
(Indra),—Wherefore, brother Agastya, do you, who are my friend, treat me with disregard ? Verily, we know what is in your mind : you do not intend to give us anything.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Sāyaṇa explains that Indra spoke this utterance, and that he addressed the sage Agastya as हे भ्रातः (*he bhrātaḥ*); and he justifies Indra's brotherhood with Agastya by *haviṣpadānavarapradā-

### Printed p. 185
nābhyāṃ parasparopakārakatvāt*—it arises from rites of mutual benefit: Indra's granting a boon and the sage Agastya's offering the oblation to Indra.
*sakhā san naḥ kiṃ atimanyase*—this sentence shows the reproach Indra makes. When there is mutual benefit between us two, you (Agastya), अस्मद्धितकार्य्यपि सर् (*asmaddhitakāryyapi sar*), though engaged in acting for our good, conceal the intention you hold concerning us. कस्मादपराधमतिक्रम्य मनुषे (*kasmādaparādhamatikramya manuṣe*): you think that you have committed no offence. If, as you said before, trust is placed in your word, our share of the oblation will be offered to the Maruts. Because of this you commit an excess against me and impute it to me as my offence (that Indra has left the Maruts without oblation); this meaning is understood from the word अपलपसि (*apalapasi*).
The intention set out in the preceding Ṛk exposes Indra's fault and, as an offence committed by Agastya against Indra, is explained as नायं अपलापः किंतु तथ्यमेव (*nāyaṃ apalāpaḥ kiṃtu tathyameva*); the reply to it is said to be given by the next sentence.
गन्तव्यं, ते मनः यथा विद्म— (*gantavyaṃ, te manaḥ yathā vidma—*)—by the word गंतव्यं (*gaṃtavyaṃ*) is suggested the attitude that Indra, knowing the sage Agastya's intention, either accepted it or disregarded and rejected it. It is explained as: all the intention of your mind is fully known to me. That intention of your mind, अस्मभ्यमिन्न दित्ससि (*asmabhyaminna ditsasi*), is that you are not willing to prepare the oblation for us and offer it to us; by the sentence अस्मदर्थं निरुप्तमस्मभ्यं दातुं नेच्छस्येव (*asmadarthaṃ niruptamasmabhyaṃ dātuṃ necchasyeva*), which teaches this meaning, Indra maintains that your unwillingness to give us what was set apart for us is your offence.

##### Grammar

- मन्यसे — मन ज्ञाने धातुः । दिवादिः । लट् मध्यमपुरुष एकवचनांतरूपम् । तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति ।
- विद्म — विद ज्ञाने धातुः । अदादिः । लट् उत्तमपुरुष बहुवचने मस् प्रत्ययः । विदो लटो वा (पा. सू. ३-४-८३) इति तस्य मादेशः । अदिप्रभृतिभ्यः शपः इति शपः लुक् । पादादित्वात् निघातस्वरः न भवति । प्रत्ययस्वरेण अंतोदात्तः भवति । द्व्यचोतस्तिजः (पा.सू. ६-३-१३५) इति संहितायां दीर्घः भवति ।

### Printed p. 186
- दित्त्वसि — डुदाञ् दाने धातुः । धातोः कर्मणः स-(पा. सू. ३-१-७) इति इच्छार्थे सन् । सन्यजोः इति धातोः द्वित्वम् । सनिमीमाघुरभ-(पा. सू. ७-४-५४) इति धातोः आकारस्य इसादेशः । अत्रलोपोभ्यासस्य (पा. सू. ७-४-५८) इति अभ्यासस्य लोपः । सः स्यार्धधातुके (पा. सू. ७-४-४९) इति पूर्वसकारस्य तकारादेशः । अतिजंतस्य परत्वात् निघातस्वरः भवति ।

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अरं॑ कृण्वंतु॒ वेदिं॒ सम॒ग्नि मिं॑धतां पु॒रः ।  
> तत्रा॒मृत॑स्य॒ चेत॑नं य॒ज्ञं ते॑ तनवावह्यॆ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अरं । कृण्वंतु । वेदिं । सं । अग्निं । इंधतां । पुरः ।  
> तत्र । अमृतस्य । चेतनं । यज्ञं । ते । तनवावहै ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इदमिंद्रवाक्यं । अपरेऽगस्त्यवाक्यमाहुः । हे यत्विजो यूयं अगस्योऽ
> द्गीकृतवान् तस्माद्वेदिमरंकृण्वंतु । संमार्जनपर्युक्षणादिना परिचरंतु । तथा
> पुरः पुरस्तादग्नि माहवनीयादिकं संमिंधतां । इद्धप्रक्षेपेण प्रज्वालयंतु ।
> शत्राग्नावमृतस्यामरणलक्षणस्य वेदत्वस्य चेतनं प्रज्ञापकं यज्ञं हे अगस्त्य ते
> त्वदीयं त्वं चाहं च तनवावहै ॥ अगस्त्यवाक्यपक्षे शिष्टमवशिष्टं । तत्र वेद्याममृतस्य तत्साधनस्य हविषो वा चेतनं प्रज्ञापनमासादनं कुरुत । आवां
> पत्नीयजमानौ अध्वर्युयजमान् ना हे इंद्र ते त्वदर्थं यज्ञं तनवावहै ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is Indra's speech; others say it is Agastya's. "O priests, since Agastya has made the sacrifice (?) of you, let them make the altar ready": let them attend to it by sweeping, sprinkling and the like. Likewise let them kindle in front the Āhavanīya and other fires by putting in fuel. **NOTE**. There, in that fire, the sacrifice that gives knowledge (cetana) of the immortal, that is, of the state of not dying, "I and you, O Agastya, shall perform, yours". On the view that it is Agastya's speech, शिष्टमवशिष्टं (*śiṣṭamavaśiṣṭaṃ*): on the altar make the placing (āsādana) of the immortal, or of the oblation that is its means. "We two, the wife and the sacrificer, or the adhvaryu and the sacrificer, O Indra, shall perform the sacrifice for you."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (हे ऋत्विजः (*he ṛtvijaḥ*))—(O priests, you)
- वेदिं (*vediṃ*)—the altar ground
- अरं कृण्वंतु (*araṃ kṛṇvaṃtu*)—adorn by sweeping and the like
- पुरः (*puraḥ*)—in front
- अग्निं (*agniṃ*)—the Āhavanīya and other fires
- सं इंधंतु (*saṃ iṃdhaṃtu*)—kindle with fuel and the like
- (ऎलै अगस्त्यने (*èlai agastyane*))—(O Agastya)
- तत्र (*tatra*)—in that kindled fire
- अमृतस्य (*amṛtasya*)—of the immortal (of knowledge)
- चेतनं (*cetanaṃ*)—that makes known (and)
- ते (*te*)—your

### Printed p. 187
- ते (*te*)—(your) related
- यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*)—the sacrifice
- तनवावहै (*tanavāvahai*)—let us perform
- [तत्र (*tatra*)—on the altar ground. अमृतस्य (*amṛtasya*)—of the oblation that brings about immortality. चेतनं (*cetanaṃ*)—having made the tasting of it (having prepared the oblation). (आवां (*āvāṃ*)—the sacrificer: I and my wife, or the two of us, priest and sacrificer.) ते (*te*)—for you. यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*)—the sacrifice. तनवावहै (*tanavāvahai*)—we perform.]

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O priests, adorn the altar ground and kindle the Āhavanīya and the other fires with fuel-sticks and the like. In that fire, O Agastya, let me (Indra) and you together perform the sacrifice that makes known immortality. [Or else: on that altar ground prepare also the oblations and other things that are the means of immortality. Then, O Indra, I, Agastya, or I and my wife, or I and the priests together, perform the sacrifice with you as its object.]

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
(Agastya),—Let the priests decorate the altar ; let them kindle the fire to the east ; and then let us both consummate the sacrifice, the inspirer of immortal (wisdom).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The words अरं कृण्वंतु वेदिं, समग्निमिंधतां पुरः, यज्ञं ते तनवावहै । (*araṃ kṛṇvaṃtu vediṃ, samagnimiṃdhatāṃ puraḥ, yajñaṃ te tanavāvahai |*) have been explained in one way as spoken by Indra to the sage Agastya, and in a second way as spoken by the sage Agastya to Indra.

Indra addresses the priests: अगस्त्यो अंगीकृतर्वा तस्मात् वेदिं अरं कृण्वंतु (*agastyo aṃgīkṛtarvā tasmāt vediṃ araṃ kṛṇvaṃtu*)—the sage Agastya, having heard the words I spoke, has agreed to offer me the oblation; therefore adorn the sacrificial altar by sweeping and sprinkling and the like (sammārjana is sweeping, paryukṣaṇa is washing with water), and पुरस्तात् आहवनीयाग्निं इध्रप्रक्षेपेण प्रज्वालयंतु (*purastāt āhavanīyāgniṃ idhraprakṣepeṇa prajvālayaṃtu*)—make this fire blaze by offering fuel-sticks to the fire called Āhavanīya for the sake of the gods. So the meaning is explained.

अमृतस्य (*amṛtasya*)—अमरणलक्षणस्य देवत्वस्य (*amaraṇalakṣaṇasya devatvasya*). The word आमृत (*āmṛta*) is stated among the names of water (Ni. 2-14), and read among the names of gold (Ni. 2-10).
रुशद्वत्सा रुशती श्वेत्यागादारैगु कृष्णा सदनान्यस्याः । समानबंधू अमृते अनूची द्यावा वर्णं चरत आमिनाने ॥ (*ruśadvatsā ruśatī śvetyāgādāraigu kṛṣṇā sadanānyasyāḥ | samānabaṃdhū amṛte anūcī dyāvā varṇaṃ carata āmināne ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-113-2)

### Printed p. 188
Here the word अमृतॆ (*amṛtè*) is explained as "those whose nature is deathlessness", that is, those who never undergo death.
वनस्पते रशनया नियूय पिष्टतमया वयुनानि विद्वान् । वहा देवत्रा दिधिषो हवींषि प्र च दातारममृतेषु वोचः ॥ (*vanaspate raśanayā niyūya piṣṭatamayā vayunāni vidvān | vahā devatrā didhiṣo havīṃṣi pra ca dātāramamṛteṣu vocaḥ ‖*)
(Ni. 8-20)
Here the meaning अमृतेषु देवेषु (*amṛteṣu deveṣu*) is explained. Having said that it means "deathless, having the nature of gods", the greatness of the sacrifice is unfolded by the sentence अमृतस्य चेतनं यज्ञं (*amṛtasya cetanaṃ yajñaṃ*).

Since ते (*te*) is a pronoun, in Indra's speech the sacrifice is related to the sage Agastya, and the meaning is given that Indra and the sage Agastya together perform the sacrifice.

If the verse is spoken by the sage Agastya to Indra, ते (*te*) is explained as त्वदर्थं (*tvadarthaṃ*)—for Indra. वेद्याममृतस्य तत्साधनस्य हविषो वा चेतनं प्रज्ञापनं असादनं कुरुत (*vedyāmamṛtasya tatsādhanasya haviṣo vā cetanaṃ prajñāpanaṃ asādanaṃ kuruta*)—on the sacrificial altar prepare the materials for accomplishing the sacrifice that secures deathlessness, and the oblations; and आवां पत्नि यजमानौ अध्वर्युयजमान् वा यःज्ञं तनवावहै (*āvāṃ patni yajamānau adhvaryuyajamān vā yaḥjñaṃ tanavāvahai*)—the wife and the sacrificer, or the Adhvaryu and the sacrificer, together perform the sacrifice for Indra alone. So the meaning is explained.

##### Grammar

- कृण्वंतु — कृवि हिंसाकरणयोश्च धातुः । लोट् प्रथमपुरुषबहुवचनस्य अंतादेशः, उत्त्वम् । धिन्विकृण्वोरच (पा. सू. ३-१-८०) इत्यनेन अकारांतादेशः उ विकरणम् । इदित्त्वात् नुमागमः । तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति ।
- वेदिं — विद ज्ञाने धातुः । हृपिषिरुहिवृति— (उ. सू. ५५८) इत्यनेन इन् प्रत्ययः । पुगंतलघूपधस्यच इत्यनेन लघूपधगुणः । इत्यादिर्नित्यम् इत्यनेन आद्युदात्तस्वरः भवति । द्वितीयाएकवचनांतरूपम् ।
- इंधतां — ञइंधी दीप्तौ धातुः । लोट् प्रथमपुरुषबहुवचनांतरूपम् । तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति ।
- चेतनं — चिती संज्ञाने धातुः । ल्युट् प्रत्ययः । युवोरनाकौ (पा. सू -१-१) इत्यनेन अनादेशः । धातुलघूपधस्य गुणः । लिति (पा. सू ६-१-१९३) इत्यनेन प्रत्ययस्य पूर्वस्य उदात्तस्वरः भवति । **NOTE**

### Printed p. 189
- तनवावहै — तनु विस्तारे धातुः । लोट् उत्तमपुरुषद्विवचने वहिज् प्रत्ययः । टेः एत्वे प्राप्ते एतऐ (पा. सू. ३-४-९३) इत्यनेन तस्य ऐकारादेशः । आडुत्तमस्यपिच्च (पा. सू. ३-४-९२) इत्यनेन आटागमः । तनादिकृञ्भ्यउः (पा. सू. ३-१-७९) इत्यनेन उ विकरणम् । पित् परत्वात् तस्य गुणः, आवादेशः । तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति ।

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वमी॑शिषे वसुपते॒ वसू॑नां॒ त्वं मि॒त्राणां॑ मित्रपते॒ धेष्णः॑ ।  
> इंद्र॒ त्वं म॒रुद्भिः॒ सं व॑द॒स्वाध॒ प्राशा॑न ऋतु॒था ह॒वींषि॑ ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वं । ईशिषे । वसुपते । वसूनां । त्वं । मित्राणां । मित्रपते । धेषः ।  
> इंद्र । त्वं । मरुत्भिः । सं । वदस्व । अध । प्र । आशान । ऋतुथा । हवींषि ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इदमगस्त्यवाक्यं । हे वसूनां वसुपतेऽतिप्रभूतानां पुरोडाशाज्यादिधनानां स्वामिन् हे इंद्र त्वमीशिषे । ईश्वरो भवसि । सर्वस्य वृत्त्यवृत्तिभ्यां बहुत्वं
> नित्यं संबंधश्च प्रतिपद्यते । यद्वा । हे वसुपते वसूनामीशिष इति योजना ।
> द्वितीयो वसुशब्दोऽनुवादो वा । तथा हे मित्राणां मित्रपतेऽस्मदादिमित्रस्य
> नित्यं पालकेंद्रत्वं धेष्णः । सर्वस्यातिशयेन धारकोऽसि । हे इंद्र त्वमुक्तमहिमस्त्वं मरुद्धिः सं वदस्व । तैः साकं सम्यक्कृतमिति वदस्व । अधापि च त्वमृतुथा
> यतावृतौ । उपलक्षणमेतत्कालस्य । तत्तद्यागकाले हवींष्याज्यचरादीनि प्राशान
> प्रकर्षण भुंक्ष्व ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is Agastya's speech. "O lord of wealth (वसुपते (*vasupate*)), of the exceedingly abundant riches—oblation-cakes, ghee and the like—O Indra, you rule (ईशिषे (*īśiṣe*))": you are the master. By the plural and the constant connection, multiplicity and permanent relation are understood. Or else the construction is: "O lord of wealth, you rule over the riches"; the second word वसु (*vasu*) is a repetition. Likewise, "O lord of friends (मित्रपते (*mitrapate*)), you are always the protecting Indra of us and other friends, the supporter (धेष्णः (*dheṣṇaḥ*))": you are the supreme upholder of all. "O Indra, of such stated greatness, speak together (सं वदस्व (*saṃ vadasva*)) with the Maruts": say with them that it has been well done. "And further, ऋतुथा (*ṛtuthā*), in due season": this is indicative of time; at each time of sacrifice, partake abundantly of the oblations (हवींषि (*havīṃṣi*))—ghee, cooked offerings and the like (प्राशान (*prāśāna*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वसूनां वसुपते (*vasūnāṃ vasupate*)—O Indra, lord of all riches such as oblation-cakes and ghee
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- ईशिषे (*īśiṣe*)—you are lord
- वसुपते (*vasupate*)—O Indra, lord of wealth
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- वसूनां (*vasūnāṃ*)—of riches in the form of oblation-cakes, ghee and the like
- ईशिषे (*īśiṣe*)—you are lord
- मित्रपते (*mitrapate*)—O Indra, protector of all friends
- मित्राणां (*mitrāṇāṃ*)—your friends, that is, of us

### Printed p. 190
- धेष्ठ (*dheṣṭha*)—you are the strong support **NOTE**
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- मरुद्भिः (*marudbhiḥ*)—together with the Marut gods
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- सं वदस्व (*saṃ vadasva*)—say (that the rite has been well done)
- अध (*adha*)—and
- ऋतुथा (*ṛtuthā*)—following the season (at the respective time of sacrifice)
- हवींषि (*havīṃṣi*)—the oblations, ghee, cooked offerings and the like
- प्र अशान (*pra aśāna*)—eat abundantly

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you are the lord of all wealth in the form of oblation-cakes and the like, and a strong support to all friends like us. Such as you are, together with the Maruts praise our rite as well done, and in each season partake abundantly of the oblations and the rest (offered by us).

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
(Agastya),—You, Vasupati, are the lord of riches ; you, Mitrapati, are the firm stay (of us), your friends : Declare, Indra, along with the Maruts, (your approval of our acts), and partake of the oblation offered in due season.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

It is explained that this mantra is spoken by the sage Agastya in answer to Indra's words.

वसूनां वसुपते — त्वं ईशिषे— अतिप्रभूतानां पुरोडाशाज्यादिधनानां स्वार्मि त्वं ईश्वरो भवसि सर्वस्य ! यद्वा वसूनां ईशिषे ! द्वितीय वसुशब्दो अनु वादो वा (*vasūnāṃ vasupate — tvaṃ īśiṣe— atiprabhūtānāṃ puroḍāśājyādidhanānāṃ svārmi tvaṃ īśvaro bhavasi sarvasya ! yadvā vasūnāṃ īśiṣe ! dvitīya vasuśabdo anu vādo vā*)—two kinds of meaning are given for this sentence. First, Indra is the lord of the riches—oblation-cakes, ghee and the like—that exist in exceeding abundance, and is the master of all; in this meaning the first word वसु (*vasu*) conveys excess. In the second meaning Indra is the lord of the Vasus; it is stated that the second word वसु (*vasu*) is read as a repetition.

Yāska has shown by examples that the word वसु (*vasu*) is read as a repetition. Among the names of rays the word वसवः (*vasavaḥ*) is stated.
सुगा वो देवाः सुपथा अकर्म य आजग्मुः सवनमिदं जुषाणः । जक्षिनांसः पपिवांसश्च विश्वेऽस्मे धत्त वसवो वसून्वि ॥ (*sugā vo devāḥ supathā akarma ya ājagmuḥ savanamidaṃ juṣāṇaḥ | jakṣināṃsaḥ papivāṃsaśca viśve 'sme dhatta vasavo vasūnvi ‖*)
(Ni. 12-42)
Here the gods are addressed by the word वसवः (*vasavaḥ*); the second word वसु (*vasu*) is said to mean wealth.

### Printed p. 191
ज्मया अत्र वसवो रंत देवा उरावंतरिक्षे मर्जयंत शुभ्राः । अर्वाक्थ उरुज्रयः कृणुध्वं श्रोता दूतस्य जग्मुषो नो अस्यं ॥ (*jmayā atra vasavo raṃta devā urāvaṃtarikṣe marjayaṃta śubhrāḥ | arvāktha urujrayaḥ kṛṇudhvaṃ śrotā dūtasya jagmuṣo no asyaṃ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-39-3)
Here the meaning of the word वसवः (*vasavaḥ*) is explained as Agni.
वसव आदित्यरत्नयः विवसनात्तस्मात् द्युस्थानाः (*vasava ādityaratnayaḥ vivasanāttasmāt dyusthānāḥ*) (Ni. 12-42): it is stated that the word वसु (*vasu*) is used of the rays of the Sun.
सुगुरसत्सुहिरण्यः स्वस्तो बृहदस्मै वय इंद्रो दधाति । यस्त्यायंतं वसुना प्रातरित्तो मुक्तीजयेव पदिमुत्तिनाति (*sugurasatsuhiraṇyaḥ svasto bṛhadasmai vaya iṃdro dadhāti | yastyāyaṃtaṃ vasunā prātaritto muktījayeva padimuttināti*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-125-2)
Here वसुना (*vasunā*) is said to mean अन्नेन (*annena*) (with food). By the sentence वृत्तवृत्तिभ्यां बहुत्वं नित्यसंबंधश्च प्रतिपाद्यते (*vṛttavṛttibhyāṃ bahutvaṃ nityasaṃbaṃdhaśca pratipādyate*) the multiplicity of the riches, and the permanent connection of Indra with the riches, are established.

मित्रपते (*mitrapate*)—this word is explained by अस्मदादिमित्रस्य नित्यं पालक (*asmadādimitrasya nityaṃ pālaka*): the protector of friends, beginning with Agastya; and Indra is addressed by this word. त्वं धेष्यः सर्वस्यातियेन धारकोऽसि (*tvaṃ dheṣyaḥ sarvasyātiyena dhārako 'si*)—the meaning is given that Indra upholds everything more than others uphold it.

मरुद्भिः संवदस्व (*marudbhiḥ saṃvadasva*)—तैः साकं सम्यक् कृतमिति वदस्व (*taiḥ sākaṃ samyak kṛtamiti vadasva*): the wish is expressed that Indra, of abundant greatness, together with the Marut gods, may say that the sacrifice has been properly and rightly performed.

ऋतुथा हवींषि प्राशान (*ṛtuthā havīṃṣi prāśāna*)—ऋतावृत् तत्तद्यागकाले अज्यचर्वादीनि प्रकर्षेण भुंक्ष्य (*ṛtāvṛt tattadyāgakāle ajyacarvādīni prakarṣeṇa bhuṃkṣya*): in each season, that is, at each time of sacrifice, may Indra come to the sacrifice and eat abundantly the oblations of ghee and the like offered in the fire. So the wish is expressed.

##### Grammar

- ईशिषे — ईश ऐश्वर्ये धातुः । लट् मध्यमपुरुषएकवचनस्य से आदेशः । ईशः से (पा. सू. ७-२-७७) इत्यनेन तस्य इडागमः । प्रत्ययस्य षत्वम् । तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति ।
- धेष्ठः — धातृ इति तृजंतात् तुत्थंदसि (पा. सू. ५-३-५९) इत्यनेन अतिशयार्थे इष्टन् प्रत्ययः । तुरिष्येमेयः सु (पा. सू. ६-४-१५४) इत्यनेन तृचः लोपः । प्रकृत्यैकाज् सूत्रेण प्रकृतिभावस्य आगमनात् टेः लोपः न भवति । गुणः । प्रत्ययस्य नित्त्वात् अद्युदात्तस्वरः भवति ।

### Printed p. 192
- वदस्व — वद व्यक्तायां वाचि धातुः । व्यक्तवाचां समुच्चारणे (पा.सू. १-३-४८) इत्यनेन आत्मनेपदी भवति । लोट् मध्यमपुरुषएकवचनांतरूपम् । अतिजंतस्य परत्वात् निघातस्वरः भवति ।
- अशान — अश भोजने धातुः । लोट् मध्यमपुरुषएकवचने सिपः हि आदेशः । क्र्यादिभ्यः श्ना (पा. सू. ३-१-८१) इत्यनेन श्ना विकरणम् । हलःश्नः शानज्ञौ (पा. सू. ३-१-८३) इत्यनेन विकरणस्य शानचादेशः । अतोहेः (पा. सू. ६-४-१०५) सूत्रेण हेः लुक् । तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति ।
- ऋतुथा — कालार्थे आवृत्तौ प्रतीयमानायां था प्रत्ययः ।

Here the one hundred and seventieth sūkta ends.

## Sūkta 171 (Maṇḍala 1)
The hundred and seventy-first sūkta.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्रति व इति षडृचं सप्तमं सूक्तमागस्त्यं त्रैष्टुभं मारुतं । आद्ये द्वे
> केवलमारुते । शिष्टाश्चतस्रो मरुत्वदिंद्रदेवताकाः । प्रति वः षण्मारुतं तु
> चतस्रोऽन्ता मरुत्वतीया इत्यनुक्रमणिका । विशेषविनियोगो लैंगिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

प्रति व इति षडृचं सप्तमं सूक्त (*prati va iti ṣaḍṛcaṃ saptamaṃ sūkta*): this is a sūkta of six ṛks, the seventh, with Agastya as seer, in the Triṣṭubh metre, addressed to the Maruts. The first two are purely addressed to the Maruts; the remaining four have the Maruts and Indra accompanied by the Maruts as deities. So says the Anukramaṇikā. The special ritual use is by the Liṅga (indication of the text itself).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning प्रति व एना (*prati va enā*) is the seventh sūkta in the twenty-third anuvāka. It has six ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Agastya; the deity is the Maruts; the metre is Triṣṭubh. The first two ṛks are addressed to the Maruts alone; the remaining four ṛks are addressed to the Maruts and Indra. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: प्रति वः षण्मारुतं तु चतस्रोऽन्ता मरुत्वतीया (*prati vaḥ ṣaṇmārutaṃ tu catasro 'ntā marutvatīyā*). The special ritual use of this sūkta is by the Liṅga.

##### Particulars

Sūkta 171. Maṇḍala 1; Anuvāka 23; Sūkta 171. Aṣṭaka 2; Adhyāya 4; Varga 11. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 6. Seer: Agastya. Deity: 1-2 **NOTE** the Maruts; 3-6 Marutvān Indra. Metre: Triṣṭubh.

### Printed p. 193
In the Bṛhaddevatā the last 27 sūktas of the first Maṇḍala are divided into two groups. From sūkta 165 to 170 it tells, in the form of a story, of the conversation between Indra and the Maruts. It also tells how the seer Agastya stepped in between them, praised the Maruts and Indra, and pacified them. Sūktas 171 to 191 were composed in the setting of the relation and the love between Agastya and Lopāmudrā. The nature of the conversation between Indra and the Maruts, and how it is described in the Bṛhaddevatā, has been explained earlier (Ṛgveda Saṃhitā, Part 12, p. 679).

स तानभिजगामाशु निरूप्यैंद्रं हविस्तदा । मरुतश्चाभितुष्टाव सूक्तैस्तन्निविति च त्रिभिः ॥ (*sa tānabhijagāmāśu nirūpyaiṃdraṃ havistadā | marutaścābhituṣṭāva sūktaistanniviti ca tribhiḥ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 4-48).

As soon as Agastya had grasped their conversation, he too went towards them and prepared the oblation for Indra. At the same time he composed three sūktas (1-166 to 168), containing the mantras beginning वोचाम (*vocāma*) and the rest, and praised the Maruts. This is the account given. And:

महच्चिदिति चैवेंद्रं सहस्रमिति चैतया । निरुप्तं तद्धहिश्चैंद्रं मरुद्भो दारुमिच्छति ॥ (*mahacciditi caiveṃdraṃ sahasramiti caitayā | niruptaṃ taddhahiścaiṃdraṃ marudbho dārumicchati ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 4-49).

With the mantra महश्चित् (*mahaścit*) (1-169) he praised Indra. With the mantra सहस्रं (*sahasraṃ*) (1-167-1) he made ready to offer to the Maruts also the oblation that had been set apart for Indra. This matter is told in the Nirukta as follows:

अगस्त्य इंद्राय हविर्निरुप्य मरुद्भः संप्रदिस्सितांचकार । स इंद्र एत्य परिदेवयांचक्रे (*agastya iṃdrāya havirnirupya marudbhaḥ saṃpradissitāṃcakāra | sa iṃdra etya paridevayāṃcakre*) (Ni. 1-5).

Displeased at this, Indra objected to it.

विज्ञायावेक्ष्य तद्भावं इंद्रो नेति तमब्रवीत् । न श्वो नाद्यतनं ह्यस्ति वेद कस्तद्यदद्भुतं ॥ (*vijñāyāvekṣya tadbhāvaṃ iṃdro neti tamabravīt | na śvo nādyatanaṃ hyasti veda kastadyadadbhutaṃ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 4-50).

Indra understood this intention and refused with न नूनमस्ति (*na nūnamasti*) (1-170). But since he refused without stating his purpose, Agastya questions him.

कस्यचित्त्वर्थसंचारे चित्तमेव विनश्यति । किं न इत्यब्रवीदिंद्रं अगस्तो भ्रातरस्तव ॥ (*kasyacittvarthasaṃcāre cittameva vinaśyati | kiṃ na ityabravīdiṃdraṃ agasto bhrātarastava ‖*)

### Printed p. 194
मरुद्भिः संप्रकल्पस्व वधीर्मा नः शतक्रतो । किं नो भ्रातरिति त्वस्यां इंद्रो मान्यमुपालभत् ॥ (*marudbhiḥ saṃprakalpasva vadhīrmā naḥ śatakrato | kiṃ no bhrātariti tvasyāṃ iṃdro mānyamupālabhat ‖*)

अगस्त्यस्वरमित्यस्यां क्षुब्धमिंद्रं प्रशामयत् । प्रादात्संवननं कृत्वा तेभ्य एव च तद्धविः ॥ (*agastyasvaramityasyāṃ kṣubdhamiṃdraṃ praśāmayat | prādātsaṃvananaṃ kṛtvā tebhya eva ca taddhaviḥ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 4-51 to 53).

Agastya indicates that in this refusal, not stating your purpose will serve no end. With the mantra किं नः (*kiṃ naḥ*) (1-170-2) he tells Indra that the Maruts too are his friends. Then, when he entreats Indra with the mantra किं नो भ्रातः (*kiṃ no bhrātaḥ*), Indra grows somewhat angry and reproaches him. Even so, with the mantra आरं (*āraṃ*) (1-170-4) Agastya pacified Indra and offered a share of the oblation to the Maruts as well.

सुते चकार सोमेऽथ तानिंद्रः सोमपीथिनः । शस्माद्विद्यान्निपातेन ऐंद्रेषु मरुतः स्तुतान् ॥ (*sute cakāra some 'tha tāniṃdraḥ somapīthinaḥ | śasmādvidyānnipātena aiṃdreṣu marutaḥ stutān ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 4-54).

As soon as the Soma was pressed, Indra divided it, gave a share also to the Maruts and made them drink it. Therefore, whatever sūkta Indra is praised in, it is to be understood that the Maruts too are praised, incidentally, together with him.

प्रीतात्मा पुनरेवर्षिस्तांस्तुष्टाव पृथक् पृथक् । मरुतः प्रति सूक्ताभ्यां इंद्रं षड्भिः परैस्तु सः ॥ (*prītātmā punarevarṣistāṃstuṣṭāva pṛthak pṛthak | marutaḥ prati sūktābhyāṃ iṃdraṃ ṣaḍbhiḥ paraistu saḥ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 4-55).

By this, Agastya, satisfied, praised the Maruts separately with two sūktas (1-171 and 1-172), and praised Indra with the six sūktas from 173 to 178.

स्तुतश्च तसृभिच्छेंद्र स्तुतास इति त्रैः सह । मरुद्भिः सह मरुत्वांस्तत्र सोऽभवत् ॥ (*stutaśca tasṛbhiccheṃdra stutāsa iti traiḥ saha | marudbhiḥ saha marutvāṃstatra so 'bhavat ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 4-56).

In four mantras (173-3 to 6) there is praise of Indra. But since the Maruts must be present wherever Indra is, the epithet मरुत्वान् (*marutvān*) is natural to him. After telling this and more in the Bṛhaddevatā, it goes on to describe the loving conversation that took place between Agastya and Lopāmudrā.

### Printed p. 195
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्रति॑ व ए॒ना नम॑सा॒हमे॑मि सू॒क्तेन॑ भिक्षे सुम॒तिं तु॒राणां॑ ।  
> र॒रा॒णता॑ मरुतो वे॒द्याभि॒र्नि हेळो॑ ध॒त्त वि मु॑चध्व॒मश्वा॑न् ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्रति । वः । एना । नमसा । अहं । एमि । सुउक्तेन । भिक्षे । सुमतिं । तुराणां ।  
> रराणता । मरुतः। वेद्याभिः । नि । हेळः । धत्त । वि । मुचध्वं । अश्वान् ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मरुतः वो युष्मानहं प्रत्येमि । अभिगच्छामि । केन साधनेन । नमसा
> नमस्कारेण हविषा वा । नम इत्यन्ननाम । तथा सूक्तेन शोभनवचनेन स्तुतिरूपेण । तुराणां त्वरमाणानां शतृणां हिंसकानां वा युष्माकं समतिं
> शोभनमतिमनुग्रहबुद्धिं भिक्षॆ । याचे । इंद्रस्यॆव पुनर्हविष्ट्रदानादिदं
> याच्यते । किंच हे मरुतः यूयं वेद्याभिर्वेदितव्याभिः स्तुतिभी रराणता
> रममाणेन मनसेत्थंभूताः संतो हेळः । क्रोधनामैतत् । क्रोधं
> युष्मदभिक्रमजनितं नि धत्त । निकृष्टं धारयत । त्यजतेत्यर्थः । तथाश्वान्विःमुचध्वं ।
> रथेभ्यो वियोजयत । क्रोधात्पुनर्गमनं मा कुरतेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Maruts, I approach you." By what means? नमसा (*namasā*) is glossed as with obeisance, or with an oblation; नम (*nama*) is a name for food. Also with a सूक्तेन (*sūktena*), that is, with fair words in the form of praise. I beg समतिं (*samatiṃ*), a good mind, a mind of favour, of you who are तुराणां (*turāṇāṃ*), that is, swift, or else destroyers of foes. This is asked as though because Indra again gave away the oblation. Further, "O Maruts, being thus delighted in mind by the praises that deserve to be known (वेद्याभिः (*vedyābhiḥ*)), set down (नि धत्त (*ni dhatta*)) your हेळः (*heḷaḥ*)." हेळ (*heḷa*) is a name for anger. The sense is: lay aside the anger born of your coming against us, cast it away. Likewise unyoke the horses (वि मुचध्वं (*vi mucadhvaṃ*)), release them from the chariots; the sense is that out of anger you should not go away again.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (हे) मरुतः (*(he) marutaḥ*)—O Marut gods
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- एना नमसा (*enā namasā*)—with this obeisance or oblation (accompanied)
- वः (*vaḥ*)—you
- प्रति एमि (*prati emi*)—come towards
- सूक्तेन (*sūktena*)—with auspicious words (in the form of praise)
- तुराणां (*turāṇāṃ*)—(in regard to) enemies who are in haste, or of you who are destroyers
- सुमतिं (*sumatiṃ*)—a mind of favour (towards us)
- भिक्षॆ (*bhikṣè*)—I beg
- वेद्याभिः (*vedyābhiḥ*)—by (our) praises
- रराणता (*rarāṇatā*)—with a delighted mind (being endowed)
- हेळः (*heḷaḥ*)—your anger
- नि धत्त (*ni dhatta*)—put down, lay aside (abandon)
- अश्वान् (*aśvān*)—the horses (of the chariot)
- वि मुचध्वं (*vi mucadhvaṃ*)—unyoke (from the chariot, so that you, filled with anger, do not come back)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Maruts, I approach you with obeisance; I praise you and beg that you show favour to us. O Maruts, made glad in mind by our praise, abandon your anger; and unyoke the horses of your chariots, so that you do not come to us in anger.

### Printed p. 196

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
I approach you, Maruts, with this reverential homage, and with a hymn implore your favour against eager (foes) : with minds pacified by our praises, suppress your anger, and let loose your horses.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

नमसा—नमस्कारेण हविषा वा (*namasā—namaskāreṇa haviṣā vā*): नमः (*namaḥ*) is read also among the names for food (Ni. 3-9). The meaning is explained that the seer Agastya will delight the Maruts by obeisance to them and by offering the oblation.

सूक्तेन तुराणां सुमतिं भिक्षे (*sūktena turāṇāṃ sumatiṃ bhikṣe*)—शोभनवचनेन स्तुतिरूपेण त्वरमाणानां शत्रोणां हिंसकानां वा (*śobhanavacanena stutirūpeṇa tvaramāṇānāṃ śatroṇāṃ hiṃsakānāṃ vā*). The word तुर (*tura*) is cited by Yāska in this Ṛk:

प्रातर्जितं भगमुग्रं हुवेम वयं पुत्रमदितेयो विधर्ता । आध्रश्चिद्यं मन्यमानस्तु रच्चिद्राजा चिद्यं भगं भक्षीत्याह ॥ (*prātarjitaṃ bhagamugraṃ huvema vayaṃ putramaditeyo vidhartā | ādhraścidyaṃ manyamānastu raccidrājā cidyaṃ bhagaṃ bhakṣītyāha ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-41-2).

On it he explains: तुर इति यमनाम तरतेर्वा त्वरतेर्वा (*tura iti yamanāma taratervā tvaratervā*). The word तुर (*tura*), formed from the root तृ (*tṛ*) or the root त्वर (*tvara*), denotes Yama, who is swift in bringing living beings to their end. Sāyaṇa gives this word the sense of those who injure, or enemies. His account is this: when the seer Agastya intended to offer to the Maruts the oblation prepared for Indra, Indra took it himself; so Agastya bows to the swift Maruts, offers the oblation, praises them with excellent words, and prays that their शोभनमतिं (*śobhanamatiṃ*), the mind of favour, the mind that shows a favour expressing delight, may arise towards himself, and that the Maruts may not act towards him as enemies or injurers (that is, may not be angry). The word तुर (*tura*) is also used in the sense of power.

सदिद्दि ते तुविजातस्य मन्ये सहः सहिष्ठ तुरतस्तु रस्य । उग्रमुग्रस्य तवसस्तवीयोऽरध्रस्य रध्रतुरो बभूव ॥ (*sadiddi te tuvijātasya manye sahaḥ sahiṣṭha turatastu rasya | ugramugrasya tavasastavīyo 'radhrasya radhraturo babhūva ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-18-4).

In this Ṛk the word is used both in the sense of power and in the sense of injuring. The words तुरतः (*turataḥ*) and तुरस्य (*turasya*) are explained as अस्मान् हिंसतः; शत्रूणां हिंसितुः बलवतो वा (*asmān hiṃsataḥ; śatrūṇāṃ hiṃsituḥ balavato vā*). It is used in the first half as an epithet of Indra, "able to subdue injuring enemies"; and in the second half, रध्रतुरः—वशीकरणीयानां सपत्नानां हिंसकस्य तव बलं (*radhraturaḥ—vaśīkaraṇīyānāṃ sapatnānāṃ hiṃsakasya tava balaṃ*), "the strength of you, the destroyer of enemies". And:

### Printed p. 197
यं वर्धयंतीद्गिरः पतिं तुरस्य राधसः । तमिन्नवस्य रोदसी देवी शुष्मं सपर्यतः ॥ (*yaṃ vardhayaṃtīdgiraḥ patiṃ turasya rādhasaḥ | taminnavasya rodasī devī śuṣmaṃ saparyataḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-44-5).

Here it is explained as "the enemy of the mighty one". Moreover:

ता वामेषे रथानामिंद्राग्नी हवामहे । पशी तुरस्य राधसो विद्वांसा गिर्वणस्तमा ॥ (*tā vāmeṣe rathānāmiṃdrāgnī havāmahe | paśī turasya rādhaso vidvāṃsā girvaṇastamā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-86-4).

In such Ṛks, तुरस्य राधसः (*turasya rādhasaḥ*) is explained as जंगमरूपस्य धनस्य (*jaṃgamarūpasya dhanasya*), "of moving, unstable wealth"; it is used as an epithet of things that are movable, fit for dealings and quick in moving about. In Ṛks such as (1-61-13) it is used precisely as a synonym of Yama. In the present place it is an epithet of the Maruts, "destroyers of foes".

The word सुमति (*sumati*) is cited by Yāska in:

धाता ददातु दाशुषे प्राचीं जीवातुमक्षितां । वयं देवस्य धीमहि सुमतिं सत्यधर्मणः ॥ (*dhātā dadātu dāśuṣe prācīṃ jīvātumakṣitāṃ | vayaṃ devasya dhīmahi sumatiṃ satyadharmaṇaḥ ‖*) (Atha. Saṃ. 7-17-2).

Sāyaṇa explains that in this Ṛk, as in other places, it denotes कल्याणी मतिः (*kalyāṇī matiḥ*).

But the word सुमति (*sumati*) is used not merely for a divine, favouring mind; it is also used in the sense of a man's prayer, or a man's excellent wish. For example:

तुभ्यं भरंति क्षितयो यविष्ठ बलिमग्ने अंतित ओत दूरात् । आ भंदिष्ठस्य सुमतिं चिकिद्दि बृहत्ते अग्ने महि शर्म भद्रं ॥ (*tubhyaṃ bharaṃti kṣitayo yaviṣṭha balimagne aṃtita ota dūrāt | ā bhaṃdiṣṭhasya sumatiṃ cikiddi bṛhatte agne mahi śarma bhadraṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-1-10).

प्र वो महे महिवृधे भरध्वं प्रचेतसे प्र सुमतिं कृणुध्वं । विशः पूर्वीः प्र चरा चर्षणिप्राः ॥ (*pra vo mahe mahivṛdhe bharadhvaṃ pracetase pra sumatiṃ kṛṇudhvaṃ | viśaḥ pūrvīḥ pra carā carṣaṇiprāḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-31-10).

In such Ṛks it is explained as स्तोतुः स्तुतिं आ जानीहि (*stotuḥ stutiṃ ā jānīhi*), "listen to the excellent praise of the praiser", and इंद्राय सुष्टुतिं प्र कुरुत (*iṃdrāya suṣṭutiṃ pra kuruta*), "recite an excellent praise addressed to Indra". And:

यूयं न उग्रा मरुतः सुचेतुनारिष्टग्रामाः सुमतिं पिपर्तन । यत्रा वो विद्युद्रदति क्रिविदर्ती रिणाति पश्वः सुधितेव बर्हणा ॥ (*yūyaṃ na ugrā marutaḥ sucetunāriṣṭagrāmāḥ sumatiṃ pipartana | yatrā vo vidyudradati krividartī riṇāti paśvaḥ sudhiteva barhaṇā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-166-6).

### Printed p. 198
In this Ṛk, explained earlier, Sāyaṇa explains सुमतिं पिपर्तन (*sumatiṃ pipartana*) as शोभनबुद्धिं पूरयत (*śobhanabuddhiṃ pūrayata*). Here "good mind" cannot mean the favouring mind of the gods. Since पिपर्तन (*pipartana*) is used, the sense "fulfil our excellent desires" is natural. Giving an explanation of this same Ṛk 1-166-6, the scholar Max Müller says:

In our passage the Verb पिपर्तन (*pipartana*), fill or fulfil, indicates in what sense सुमति (*sumati*) ought to be taken. सुमतिं पिपर्तन (*sumatiṃ pipartana*) is no more than कामं पिपर्तन (*kāmaṃ pipartana*). [S. B. E. XXXII. P. 221].

That is, it means "fulfil our desire". This sense too is natural. But in the present Ṛk, सुमतिं भिक्षॆ (*sumatiṃ bhikṣè*) means "I desire the favouring mind of the Maruts".

Addressing the Maruts with हे मरुतः (*he marutaḥ*), the explanation given is that they, वेद्याभिः रराणता (*vedyābhiḥ rarāṇatā*), knowing Agastya's wish through the praises he has recited, should be रममाणेन मनसा (*ramamāṇena manasā*), of a mind filled with delight.

रराणता (*rarāṇatā*): this word is formed from the root रण शब्दे (*raṇa śabde*). Ordinarily it means a sound in the form of praise. But in some contexts the word is used also in the sense of delight. Quoting the Ṛk in which this word is used,

नाहमिंद्राणि रारण सख्युर्वृषाकपेर्यते । यस्येदमप्यं हविः प्रियं देवेषु गच्छति विश्वस्मादिंद्र उत्तरः ॥ (*nāhamiṃdrāṇi rāraṇa sakhyurvṛṣākaperyate | yasyedamapyaṃ haviḥ priyaṃ deveṣu gacchati viśvasmādiṃdra uttaraḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-86-12 **NOTE**)

the author of the Nirukta gives रारण (*rāraṇa*) the sense रमे (*rame*). In some places Sāyaṇa gives only the sense of sound. For example:

यः सोम सख्ये तव, कारणद्देव मर्त्यः । तं दक्षः सचले कविः ॥ (*yaḥ soma sakhye tava, kāraṇaddeva martyaḥ | taṃ dakṣaḥ sacale kaviḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-91-14).

In such Ṛks he explains रणति एतत्तूक्तरूपेण त्वां स्तौति (*raṇati etattūktarūpeṇa tvāṃ stauti*) as "they praise you with this sūkta-form (word)". For the most part, use in the sense of delight is the more frequent.

हेळः निधत्त अश्वान् वि मुचध्वं (*heḷaḥ nidhatta aśvān vi mucadhvaṃ*): the word हेळस् (*heḷas*) is read among the names for anger (Ni. 3-9). The Maruts should युष्मदतिक्रमजनितं क्रोधं निकृष्टं धारयत त्यजत (*yuṣmadatikramajanitaṃ krodhaṃ nikṛṣṭaṃ dhārayata tyajata*).

### Printed p. 199
…the Maruts are prayed to give up the anger that has arisen in them because they were transgressed against. And "do not go back in anger": let the horses be unyoked from the chariots made ready to come to the sacrifice, and let them not turn back in anger. This is the prayer of the sage Agastya stated here; so the meaning has been described.

##### Grammar

- नमसा—णमु प्रह्वत्वे शब्दे च धातुः । औणादिकः असुन् प्रत्ययः । नित्त्वात् आद्युदात्तस्वरः भवति ।
- एमि—इण् गत्. धातुः । लट् उत्तमपुरुष एकवचनांतरूपम् । तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति ।
- सूक्तेन—वच परिभाषणे. धातुः । क्तप्रत्ययः । वचिस्वपियजादीनां किति (पा. सू. ६-१-१५) इति संप्रसारणं पूर्वरूपं च । नञ्सुभ्याम् (पा.सू. ६-२-१७२) इति अंतोदात्तस्वरः भवति ।
- भिक्षे—भिक्ष याज्ञायां. धातुः । लट् उत्तमपुरुष एकवचनांतरूपम्, तिजंत निघात स्वरः भवति ।
- सुमतिम्—मन ज्ञाने. मंत्रे वृषेषपच—(पा. सू. ३-३-९६) इति क्तिन् उदात्तः भवति । कृदुत्तरपद प्रकृतिस्वरः भवति ।
- तुराणां—तुर त्वर्णे, धातुः, इगुपधज्ञा प्रीकिरः कः (पा. सू. ३-१-१५५) इति क प्रत्ययः । कित्त्वात् लघोपधगुणः न भवति । अथवा तुर्वी हिंसायां धातुः । पचादिषु अंतर्भूतत्वात् अट् प्रत्ययः । छांदसः वकारलोपः । प्रत्यय स्वरेण अंतोदात्तः भवति । षष्ठि बहुवचनांतरूपम् ।
- रराणता—रमु क्रीडायां धातुः । अस्मात् लडर्थे शानच् । बहुलं छंदसि इति श्लु निमित्तेन धातोः द्वित्वे प्राप्ते व्यत्ययेन धातु मकारस्य अत्वम् । तृतीया टा परे तकारः अधिकः आगच्छति ।
- धत्त—डुधाञ् धारणपोषणयोः धातुः । लोट् मध्यमपुरुष बहुवचनांतरूपम्, पादादौ स्थितत्वात् निघातस्वरः न भवति । प्रत्ययस्वरेण अंतोदात्तः भवति ।
- मुचध्वं—मुच्‌ल्य मोक्षणे. धातुः । तुदादिः । लोट् मध्यमपुरुष बहुवचने ध्वम् प्रत्ययः । टेः एत्वे प्राप्ते सवाभ्यांवाम् (पा. सू. ३-४-९१) इति अमादेशः । तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति । ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 200
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ए॒ष वः॒ स्तोमो॑ मरुतो॒ नम॑स्वान्हृ॒दा त॒ष्टो मन॑सा धायि  
> देवाः ।  
> उपे॒मा या॑त॒ मन॑सा जुषा॒णा यू॒यं हि ष्ठा नम॑स॒  ॒॑
> इद्वधासः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> एषः । वः । स्तोमः । मरुतः । नमस्वान् । हृदा । तष्टः । मनसा । धायि. । देवाः ।  
> उप । ईं । आ । यात । मनसा । जुषाणाः । यूयं । हि । स्थ । नमसः । इत् । वृधासः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मरुतः एष वः स्तोम इदानीं क्रियमाणप्रकारो वो युष्माकं ।
> युष्मदर्थ इत्यर्थः । कीदृशोऽयं । नमस्वान् । नम इत्यन्ननाम । हविर्लक्षणान्नवान् । न केवलं स्तुतिमात्रं भक्षणाय हविरपि युष्माकमित्यर्थः । किंच हृदा तप्पो
> युष्मन्निवेदनबुद्ध्या संपादितः । तादृशः स्तोमो हे देवा देवनशीलाः
> मनसास्मदनुग्रहवता चित्तेन धायि । धार्यतां । तदर्थं मनसादरया बुद्ध्या
> जुषाणा ईमेनां स्तुतिं तद्धविश्च सेवमाना उपा यात । उपागच्छतैव । मा
> विलंबं कुरुत । यूयं नमसोऽन्नस्य हवीरूपस्य वृधासः स्थ ह । वर्धयितारो
> भवथ खलु । अतो गंतव्यमेव । इत्तूरणः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Maruts, this praise of yours", the kind now being performed, is "for you", that is, made for your sake. What kind is it? नमस्वान् (*namasvān*): नम (*nama*) is a name for food, so it is accompanied by food in the form of oblations. Not praise alone: there are also oblations of yours for eating. Moreover it is "wrought by the heart", brought about with the intent of offering it to you. Let such praise, O divine ones, be held with a mind favourable to us. For that, receiving this praise and its oblation with a respectful mind, come near; do not delay. You are the increasers of food in the form of oblations; therefore you must come.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (हे) मरुतः (*(he) marutaḥ*)—O Maruts
- नमस्वान् (*namasvān*)—accompanied by homage (oblations)
- एषः स्तोमः (*eṣaḥ stomaḥ*)—this praise
- वः (*vaḥ*)—for you
- हृदा (*hṛdā*)—wholeheartedly
- तप्पः (*tappaḥ*)—has been prepared (such a praise)
- (हे) देवाः (*(he) devāḥ*)—O shining Maruts
- मनसा (*manasā*)—with a respectful mind
- धायि (*dhāyi*)—let it be held (accepted)
- यूयं (*yūyaṃ*)—you
- नमसः (*namasaḥ*)—of the food in the form of oblations
- वृधासः स्थ हि इत् (*vṛdhāsaḥ stha hi it*)—you are indeed the increasers (therefore)
- मनसा (*manasā*)—with a respectful mind
- ईं (*īṃ*)—this (offered by us: homage or praise)
- जुषाणाः (*juṣāṇāḥ*)—receiving
- उप आयात (*upa āyāta*)—come

### Printed p. 201

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Maruts, this praise, accompanied by homage (oblations), has been prepared for you wholeheartedly. Accept this praise of ours respectfully; since you are the increasers of the oblation, come with gracious hearts to accept our oblation.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
This praise, accompanied by offerings, Maruts, ir for you, offered from the heart : accept it, divinities, with favour, and come with willing minds (to receive) these (laudations), for you are the augmenters of sacrificial food. **NOTE**

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

वः स्तोमः नमस्मान् हृदा तष्टः (*vaḥ stomaḥ namasmān hṛdā taṣṭaḥ*)—इदानीं क्रियामाणप्रकारः हविर्लक्षणान्नर्वा युष्मनिवेदनबुद्ध्या संपादितः । (*idānīṃ kriyāmāṇaprakāraḥ havirlakṣaṇānnarvā yuṣmanivedanabuddhyā saṃpāditaḥ |*). Our intellect, which recites addressing the Maruts and prepares and offers the oblation for them, exists only to win their favour. Hence मनसा धायि अस्मदनुग्रहवता चित्तेन धार्यतां (*manasā dhāyi asmadanugrahavatā cittena dhāryatāṃ*): the prayer is that the shining Maruts, with a mind that shows favour to us, listen to our praises and accept our oblations. Here the words हृदा तप्पः (*hṛdā tappaḥ*) and मनसा धायि (*manasā dhāyi*) make clear the intent of the composition; they indicate its skill and its power to delight.
तष्टः (*taṣṭaḥ*)—तक्षतिः करोतिकर्मा (*takṣatiḥ karotikarmā*) (Ni. 4-19). Whenever skill in making is indicated, this word is used.
आ शे अग्न ऋचा हविर्हृदा तप्पं भरामसि (*ā śe agna ṛcā havirhṛdā tappaṃ bharāmasi*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-16-47)
हृदिस्पृशो मनसा वच्यमाना अस्मभ्यं चित्रं वृषणं रयिं दाः । (*hṛdispṛśo manasā vacyamānā asmabhyaṃ citraṃ vṛṣaṇaṃ rayiṃ dāḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-47-7)
In such places, as described there, the meaning is that a quality delighting the mind and a devout skill are joined together.
मनसा जुषाणाः ईं उपा यात (*manasā juṣāṇāḥ īṃ upā yāta*)—आदरया बुद्ध्या एनां स्तुतिं तद्धविश्चसेवमानाः मा विलंबं कुरुत । (*ādarayā buddhyā enāṃ stutiṃ taddhaviścasevamānāḥ mā vilaṃbaṃ kuruta |*). The prayer is that the Maruts, with a mind full of affection toward us who praise them, come quickly to our sacrifice in order to hear our praise and to partake of our oblation.

### Printed p. 202
Here the particle ईं (*īṃ*) is spoken of by the Nirukta authors thus: अथ ये प्रवृत्तेऽर्थेऽमिताक्षरेषु ग्रंथेषु वाक्यपूरणा आगच्छंति, पदपूरणास्ते मिताक्षरेष्टनर्थकाः कमीमिद्विति । (*atha ye pravṛtte 'rthe 'mitākṣareṣu graṃtheṣu vākyapūraṇā āgacchaṃti, padapūraṇāste mitākṣareṣṭanarthakāḥ kamīmidviti |*) (Ni. 1-9). The particles कं (*kaṃ*), ईं (*īṃ*), इत् (*it*), उ (*u*) are used in sentences to fill out the sentence, and in the metres to fill out the line. But since it is also said एवमुच्चावचेष्टर्थेषु निपतंति त उपेक्षितव्याः बहुप्रकारेषु समाहृताश्चान्येप्येवं बहुप्रकारा प्रयुज्यमाना अर्थनिर्वचनशास्त्रमुपगम्य ईक्षितव्याः (*evamuccāvaceṣṭartheṣu nipataṃti ta upekṣitavyāḥ bahuprakāreṣu samāhṛtāścānyepyevaṃ bahuprakārā prayujyamānā arthanirvacanaśāstramupagamya īkṣitavyāḥ*), that is, the use in each text according to its own meaning is to be understood following the rule of the respective śāstra, the meaning एनां स्तुतिं (*enāṃ stutiṃ*), "this praise", has been given here for the particle ईं (*īṃ*). The word इत् (*it*) is described as used for filling out the line, and then: यूयं नमसः वृधासः स्थ हि (*yūyaṃ namasaḥ vṛdhāsaḥ stha hi*), हवीरूपस्य अन्नस्य वर्धयितारो भवथ खलु (*havīrūpasya annasya vardhayitāro bhavatha khalu*). That the Maruts are increasers even of the food that takes the form of the oblation is a well-known matter.
रोदसी हि मरुतश्च क्रिरे वृधे मदंति वीरा विदथेषु फृष्टयः । (*rodasī hi marutaśca krire vṛdhe madaṃti vīrā vidatheṣu phṛṣṭayaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-85-1)
Because the Maruts are the cause of the growth of heaven and earth and the means of the increase of food, it is described in many places that through their favour sacrificers obtain comfort and joy in carrying out sacrifices and other rites and in enjoying the wealth of life. But one more point must be noticed here. In one or two places it is stated that the Maruts procure the food useful for the oblation, but do not themselves consume the oblation as the other gods do. For example:
अहुतादौ ना देवानां मरुतः (*ahutādau nā devānāṃ marutaḥ*) (Śa. Brā. 4-5-2-16)
Although it is stated emphatically that the Maruts do not eat offered food, it should be noticed that in those places "unoffered food" does not mean the oblation that is ordinarily offered. Therefore, in the present Ṛk, the meaning of वः स्तोमः नमस्वान् हृदा तष्टः (*vaḥ stomaḥ namasvān hṛdā taṣṭaḥ*) is naturally that the praise has been made together with the oblation-food.
सीदता बर्हिरुरु वः सदस्यृतं मादयध्वं मरुतो मध्वो अंधसः । (*sīdatā barhiruru vaḥ sadasyṛtaṃ mādayadhvaṃ maruto madhvo aṃdhasaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-85-6)
In such places the prayer "be gratified by drinking the sweet Soma juice" is clear.

### Printed p. 203

##### Grammar

- नमस्वान्—नमः अन्नं अस्य अस्ति इति नमस्वान् । मतुप् प्रत्ययः । माडुपधायाश्च—इति मकारस्य वत्वम् । प्रत्ययः पित्त्वात् अनुदात्तः भवति ।
- हृदा—हृदयशब्दात् तृतीया एकवचने परे पद्दन्नोमास्—(पा. सू. ६-१-६३) इति हृदादेशः । ऊडिदंपदादि—(पा. सू. ६-१-१७१) इति विभक्तेः उदात्तस्वरः भवति ।
- तष्टः—तक्षू तनूकरणे. धातुः । कर्मणि क्त प्रत्ययः । स्कोः संयोगा—इति तकारस्य लोपः । प्रत्यय स्वरेण अंतोदात्तः भवति ।
- धायि—डुधाञ् धारणपोषणयोः धातुः । कर्मणि छांदसः लुज् । चिळ्गॆ चिण् । चिणो लुक् (पा. सू. ६-४-१०४) इति त शब्दस्य लुक् । आतो युक् चिण् कृतोः (पा. सू. ७-३-३३) इति युकागमः । छांदसत्वात् अडगमः न भवति । तिजंत निघातस्वरः भवति ।
- यात—या प्रापणे, धातुः । लोट् मध्यमपुरुष बहुवचनांतरूपम् । तिजंतनिघात स्वरः भवति ।
- जुषाणाः—जुषीप्रीति सेवनयोः धातुः । लटः शानचादेशः । अट् कुप्वाज्—सूत्रेण तस्य नकारस्य णत्वम् । चितः (पा. सू. ६-१-१६३) इति अंतोदात्तस्वरः भवति ।
- स्थॆ—आस भुनि धातुः । लट् मध्यमपुरुष बहुवचनांतरूपम् । निघातस्वरः भवति ।
- वृधासः—वृधु वृद्धौ धातुः । इगुपधज्ञाप्रीकिरः कः (पा. सू. ३-१-१३५) इति त प्रत्ययः । वृध इत्यस्मात् जस् परे आज्जसेरसुक् (पा. सू. ७.१-५०) इति तस्य असुकागमः । क प्रत्ययस्वरः भवति । ॥ २ ॥

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्तु॒तासो॑ नो म॒रुतो॑ मृळयंतू॒त स्तु॒तो म॒घवा॒ शंभ॑विष्ठः ।  
> ऊ॒र्ध्वान् नः॑ संतु को॒म्या वना॒न्यहा॑नि॒ विश्वा॑ मरुतो जिगी॒षा ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 204

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> स्तुतासः । नः । मरुतः । मृळयंतु । उत । स्तुतः । मघवा । शंभविष्यः ।  
> ऊर्ध्वा । नः । संतु । कोम्या । वनानि । अहानि । विश्वा । मरुतः । जिगीषा ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ते मरुतः स्तुतासः स्तुताः संतो नोऽस्मान्मृळयंतु सुखयंतु ।
> उतापि च मघवा । मघमिति धननाम । अस्मद्दातव्यबहुधनेन तद्वानिंद्रः स्तुतः
> शंभविष्ठः सुखस्य भावियितृतमः सन्नस्मान्मृळयतु । किंच हे मरुतः नोऽस्माकं
> जिगीषा ॥ जिगातेर्गतिकर्मणो जिगीषा ॥ जिगीषितव्यानि प्राप्तव्यान्युत्तरत्रानुभूयमानानि विश्वा सर्वाण्यहानि दिवसानूर्ध्वोन्नतान्युपर्युपरि श्रिया यशसा
> वोत्कृष्टानि कोम्या काम्यानि स्पृहणीयानि वनानि सर्वैः संभजनीयानि संतु ।
> जिगीषा जेतुमिष्टानि जेतव्यानि च संत्विति वा योज्यं । एवं कृत्वा मरुत इंद्रश्च
> सुखयंत्वित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Those Maruts, being praised, make us happy. And Maghavan, Indra: *maghaṃ* is a name for wealth; he is possessed of the abundant wealth to be given to us. Praised, as the greatest bringer of happiness, let him make us happy. Further, O Maruts, जिगीषा (*jigīṣā*): from *jigāti*, a verb of motion, meaning things to be attained and later experienced. May all the days be ऊर्ध्वा (*ūrdhvā*), raised up, excelling more and more in splendour or fame, कोम्या (*komyā*), desirable and coveted, and वनानि (*vanāni*), to be shared by all. Or it is to be construed: जिगीषा (*jigīṣā*), may they be wished to be won and fit to be won. Having done so, may the Maruts and Indra make us happy: this is the meaning.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (ते) मरुतः (*(te) marutaḥ*)—those Maruts
- स्तुतासः (*stutāsaḥ*)—praised (by us)
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- मृळयंतु (*mṛḷayaṃtu*)—let them make happy
- उत (*uta*)—and
- स्तुतः (*stutaḥ*)—praised
- मघवा (*maghavā*)—Indra, endowed with wealth (to be given to us)
- शंभविष्यः (*śaṃbhaviṣyaḥ*)—as the greatest bringer of happiness
- *(mṛḷayatu)*—let him grant us happiness
- *(kiṃca)*—and
- (हे) मरुतः (*(he) marutaḥ*)—O Maruts
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- जिगीषा (*jigīṣā*)—to be attained
- विश्वा अहानि (*viśvā ahāni*)—all days
- ऊर्ध्वा (*ūrdhvā*)—excellent
- कोम्या (*komyā*)—desirable
- वनानि (*vanāni*)—causes of happiness (for all), and
- *(jigīṣā)*—worthy of being won
- संतु (*saṃtu*)—let them be (by your favour)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Maruts, praised by us, give us happiness; let Indra, lord of wealth, also being praised, bring happiness; and O Maruts, let it be so, as all wish; may we henceforth (by your favour) live happily and in the best manner.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
May the Maruts, now hymned, bestow happiness upon us : may Maghavan, now glorified, be propitious to us : Maruts, may all the

### Printed p. 205

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
ensuing days that may be expected by us prove gratifying, and full of enjoyment.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

हे मरुतः (*he marutaḥ*)—addressing the Maruts, the verse prays that they, praised by the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice, may make us happy (मृळयंतु (*mṛḷayaṃtu*)).

Yāska, at क्षेत्रस्य पते मधुमंतमूर्मिं धेनुरिव पयो अस्मासु धुक्षु । मधुश्चुतं घृतमिव सुपूतमृतस्य नः पतयो मृळयंतु ॥ (*kṣetrasya pate madhumaṃtamūrmiṃ dhenuriva payo asmāsu dhukṣu | madhuścutaṃ ghṛtamiva supūtamṛtasya naḥ patayo mṛḷayaṃtu ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-57-2), has explained the word मृळयंतु (*mṛḷayaṃtu*) in this verse as मृळयतिरुपदयाकर्म वा (*mṛḷayatirupadayākarma vā*). Durgācārya has given the meaning: मृळयंतु नित्यमस्मान् रक्षंतु पूजयंतु वा (*mṛḷayaṃtu nityamasmān rakṣaṃtu pūjayaṃtu vā*), that is, "may they protect, or may they cause us (our worship) to be honoured."

मघवा स्तुतः शंभविष्ठः (*maghavā stutaḥ śaṃbhaviṣṭhaḥ*)—the word मघ (*magha*) is listed among the names of wealth (Ni. 3-9). And मघमिति धननामधेयं महतेर्दानकर्मणः (*maghamiti dhananāmadheyaṃ mahaterdānakarmaṇaḥ*) (Ni. 1-7): Yāska has explained that the word मघ (*magha*), derived from the root मंह (*maṃha*) meaning "to give", denotes wealth. Sāyaṇa explains this word as मघमिति धननाम अस्मद्दातव्यबहुधनेन तद्वानिंद्रः (*maghamiti dhananāma asmaddātavyabahudhanena tadvāniṃdraḥ*): मघ (*magha*) means wealth, and Indra, possessing abundant wealth to be given freely to the sacrificers, is famed as Maghavan. It was stated earlier that the word मघवत् (*maghavat*) may also be applied to the sacrificers.

In some other places मघवा (*maghavā*) means Indra united with Viṣṇu. Since Indra obtains Viṣṇu's help when he fights the Asuras and others, there is Vedic authority for stating this meaning:
स उ एव मखः स विष्णुः । तत इंद्रो मखवानभवन्मखवान्ह वै तं मघवानित्या चक्षते परोक्षं परोक्षकामा हि देवाः । (*sa u eva makhaḥ sa viṣṇuḥ | tata iṃdro makhavānabhavanmakhavānha vai taṃ maghavānityā cakṣate parokṣaṃ parokṣakāmā hi devāḥ |*) (Śa. Brā. 14-1-1-13).
The Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa explains that because the sacrifice (मख (*makha*)) is Viṣṇu, and Indra is therefore मखवंत (*makhavaṃta*), the gods, who love the indirect, call him मघवंत (*maghavaṃta*). In several places the association of Indra, the Maruts and Viṣṇu is well known. For example—

### Printed p. 206
एता अग्न आरुषाणास इष्टीयुर्वोः सचाभ्यश्याम वाचान् । मेंद्रो नो विष्णुर्मरुतः परि ख्यन् यूयं पात स्वस्तिभिः सदा नः ॥ (*etā agna āruṣāṇāsa iṣṭīyurvoḥ sacābhyaśyāma vācān | meṃdro no viṣṇurmarutaḥ pari khyan yūyaṃ pāta svastibhiḥ sadā naḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-93-8).
Here Indra and the Maruts are invoked together with Viṣṇu. Besides, in some places the word मारुत (*māruta*) is indicated as an epithet of Viṣṇu. For example—
अग्न इंद्र वरुण मित्र देवाः शर्धः प्र यंत मारुतोत विष्णो । उभा नासत्या रुद्रो अध ग्नाः पूषा भगः सरस्वती जुषंत ॥ (*agna iṃdra varuṇa mitra devāḥ śardhaḥ pra yaṃta mārutota viṣṇo | ubhā nāsatyā rudro adha gnāḥ pūṣā bhagaḥ sarasvatī juṣaṃta ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-46-2)
तमस्य राजा वरुणस्तमश्विना क्रतुं सचंत मारुतस्य वेधसः ॥ (*tamasya rājā varuṇastamaśvinā kratuṃ sacaṃta mārutasya vedhasaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-156-4)
In such places, some hold that the words मारुत (*māruta*) and मारुतस्य (*mārutasya*) are epithets of Viṣṇu. Explaining just this, the scholar Max Müller has said:

Vishnu, whose character in the hymns of the Veda is very different from that assumed by him in later periods of Hindu religion, must be taken here as the friend and companion of Indra. Like Maruts, he assisted Indra in his battle against Vritra and the conquest of the clouds. When Indra was forsaken by all the Gods, Vishnu came to his help. In VII. 68. 8. Indra, Vishnu and Maruts are called upon to-gether Nay, मारुत (*māruta*), belonging to the Maruts becomes actually an epithet of Vishnu. (V. 46. 2.) and in I 156. 4, मारुतस्य (*mārutasya*) has been pointed out by Roth as an appellation of Vishnu. (S. B. E Vol. XXXII. P. 134)

But only in the view of some are these words epithets of Viṣṇu. Sāyaṇa has not stated this opinion. In the present verse the word मघवा (*maghavā*) alone may be understood either as Indra the possessor of wealth, or as Indra united with Viṣṇu.

This Indra (praised by the sacrificers), hearing the praises recited by the sacrificers, blesses abundantly the sacrificers who praised him, saying "let there be happiness" (सुखस्य भावयितृतमः (*sukhasya bhāvayitṛtamaḥ*)), and so prays: "may he make us, who perform the sacrifice, live in happiness" (अर्स्मा मृळयतु (*arsmā mṛḷayatu*)).

जिगीषा (*jigīṣā*)—जिगीषितव्यानि प्राप्तव्यानि उत्तरत्रानुभूयमानानि (*jigīṣitavyāni prāptavyāni uttaratrānubhūyamānāni*): the days that are to be reached by the sacrificers, ready to be enjoyed, or that are to pass hereafter, all the days (*viśvā

### Printed p. 207
āhāni*)—the verse hopes that they may be raised up (ऊर्ध्वा संतु यशसा श्रीया वा उन्नतानि उत्कृष्टानि (*ūrdhvā saṃtu yaśasā śrīyā vā unnatāni utkṛṣṭāni*)), each more excellent than the last through fame and through prosperity.

For the word क्योम्या (*kyomyā*): काम्यानि, स्पृहणीयानि (*kāmyāni, spṛhaṇīyāni*)—in the days to be lived through hereafter, may they be praiseworthy because all our desires are fulfilled, and वनानि (*vanāni*)—may they be such as to be gained by all.

The word वन (*vana*) is listed among the names of rays (Ni. 2-15) and among the names of water (Ni. 2-23). Besides these, this word is used in the Ṛksaṃhitā in many other senses. Since nouns and verbs such as वनानि (*vanāni*), वनामहे (*vanāmahe*), वनतं (*vanataṃ*) are formed and used either from the root वन संभक्तौ (*vana saṃbhaktau*), which gives the sense of extreme desire and longing, or from the root वनु याचने (*vanu yācane*), "to request, to pray", some examples may be given to show these differences of meaning. Among uses as nouns or adjectives:
फृषुं पावकं वनिनं विचर्षन् रुद्रस्य सूनुं हवसा गृणीमसि । (*phṛṣuṃ pāvakaṃ vaninaṃ vicarṣan rudrasya sūnuṃ havasā gṛṇīmasi |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-64-12)
here वनवंतं (*vanavaṃtaṃ*) is taken as उदकवंतं (*udakavaṃtaṃ*), so the word वन (*vana*) means water;
वनानि विभ्यो नकिरस्य तानि व्रता देवस्य सवितुर्मिनंति । (*vanāni vibhyo nakirasya tāni vratā devasya saviturminaṃti |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-38-7)
in verses such as this वनानि (*vanāni*) means trees;
पारावतस्य रातिषु द्रवच्च क्रेष्टाशुषु । शिष्ठं वनस्य मध्य आ ॥ (*pārāvatasya rātiṣu dravacca kreṣṭāśuṣu | śiṣṭhaṃ vanasya madhya ā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-34-18)
here it means a part of a chariot. Looking likewise at uses as verbs, in what is quoted in the Nirukta (Ni. 5-2):
त्वया वयं मघवन्नूर्व्ये धन इंद्रत्वोताः सासह्याम पृतन्यतो वनुयाम वनुष्यतः ॥ (*tvayā vayaṃ maghavannūrvye dhana iṃdratvotāḥ sāsahyāma pṛtanyato vanuyāma vanuṣyataḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-132-1)
and
अस्माकेभिर्नृभिर्वयं सासह्याम सृतन्यतो वनुयाम वनुष्यतो नभंतामन्यके समे ॥ (*asmākebhirnṛbhirvayaṃ sāsahyāma sṛtanyato vanuyāma vanuṣyato nabhaṃtāmanyake same ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-40-7)

### Printed p. 208
in these utterances the sense is "to kill";
कुविद्देवस्य सहसा चकानः सुम्ममग्निर्वनते वावृधानः । (*kuviddevasya sahasā cakānaḥ summamagnirvanate vāvṛdhānaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-3-10)
here the sense is "gives";
वनेम पूर्वीरयो मनीषा अग्निः सुशोको विश्वान्यश्याः । (*vanema pūrvīrayo manīṣā agniḥ suśoko viśvānyaśyāḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-70-1)
in such places वनेम (*vanema*) means "let us serve with reverence";
प्रिया आपिधीर्वनिषीष्ट मेधिर आ वनषीष्ट मेधिरः । (*priyā āpidhīrvaniṣīṣṭa medhira ā vanaṣīṣṭa medhiraḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-127-7)
here it means "may he accept with love and serve"; and so there are still some other senses. Since many meanings are thus possible, in the present verse वनानि (*vanāni*) may also be given the meaning संभजनीयानि (*saṃbhajanīyāni*).

In the second interpretation: विश्वा अहानि सर्वाणि दिवसानि जिगीषा संतु (*viśvā ahāni sarvāṇi divasāni jigīṣā saṃtu*) and जेतुमिष्टानि जेतव्यानि च संतु (*jetumiṣṭāni jetavyāni ca saṃtu*)—the meaning is described thus: may all the days that the sacrificers have to live through bring them victory. The main intention is that Indra and the Maruts also, showing this kind of favour toward the sacrificers, should keep them in happiness; and the

##### Grammar

- स्तुतासः — ष्टुञ् स्तुत् धातुः । कर्मणि क्तप्रत्ययः । जस् परे अज्ज सेरसुक् इत्यनेन असुक् आगमः ।
- मृळयंतु — मृड सुखने धातुः । लोट् प्रथमपुरुष बहुवचनांतरूपम् । तिजंतनिघातस्वरः भवति ।
- शंभविष्ठः — शं भावयिता । शंभवितृ इति तृजंतस्य उपरि तुश्चंदसि इत्यनेन अतिशयार्थे इष्टन् प्रत्ययः । तुरिष्ठेमेयःसु इत्यनेन एतस्मिन् परे तृचः लोपः । ओर्गुणः (पा. सू. ६-४-१४६) इत्यनेन उकारस्य गुणः । अवादेशः । इत्यादिर्नित्यम् इत्यनेन आद्युदात्तस्वरः भवति ।
- ऊर्ध्वा — नपुंसके शेश्छंदसि बहुलम् (पा. सू. ६-१-७०) इत्यनेन शियः लोपः । कोम्या, विश्वा, इत्यत्रापि एवं शियः लुक् ।
- कोम्या — कमु कांतौ धातुः । ऋहलोर्ण्यत् (पा. सू. ३-१-१२४) इति सूत्रेण ण्यत् प्रत्ययः । आदिवृद्धौ आगतायां छांदसत्वेन ओत्वम् । व्यत्ययेन अंतोदात्तस्वरः भवति ।

### Printed p. 209
- जिगीषा — गा स्तुत् धातुः । अत्र गत्यर्थे प्रयुक्तः । इच्छार्थे धातोः कर्मणः (पा. सू. ३-१-७) इत्यनेन सन् । व्यत्ययेन ईत्वम् । अथवा जि जये धातुः सन् परे अज्जनगमां सनि (पा. सू. ६-४-१४) इत्यनेन दीर्घः । सन्नंतस्य उपरि अच् प्रत्ययः । नपुंसके पूर्ववत् शि आदेशः आगत्य रूपे सिद्धे तस्य लोपः । **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒स्माद॒हं त॑वि॒षादीष॑माण॒ इंद्रा॑द्भि॒या म॑रुतो॒ रेज॑मानः ।  
> यु॒ष्मभ्यं॑ ह॒व्या निशि॑तान्यास॒न्तान्या॒रे च॑क्रमा मृ॒ळता॑ नः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अस्मात् । अहं । तविषात् । ईषमाणः । इंद्रात् । भिया । मरुतः । रेजमानः ।  
> युष्मभ्यं । हव्या । निशितानि । आसन् । तानि । आरे । चक्रम । मृळत । नः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मरुतः शृणुत । अहमस्मात्पसिद्धात्तविषाद्बलवत इंद्रात्तत्सकाशाद्भिया
> भीत्येषमाणः पलायमानः पुरतः स्थातुमशक्तः तथा रेजमानो वेपमानश्चाभवं । ततश्च किमायातमिति आत आह । यानि युष्मभ्यं हव्या हवींषि निशितानि
> संस्कृतान्यासन् तानि तान्येवारे चक्रम । दूरे कृतवंतो वयमिंद्रभयात् ।
> अतो नोऽस्थाननपराधिनो मृळत । मृळयत । सुखयत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Maruts, hear. I, fearing that famous, mighty Indra, and from his presence, ईषमाणः (*īṣamāṇaḥ*)—fleeing, unable to stand before him—was also trembling (रेजमानः (*rejamānaḥ*), shaking). And what came of that?" Therefore he says: the oblations (हव्या (*havyā*), हवींषि (*havīṃṣi*)) that had been prepared (निशितानि (*niśitāni*), *saṃskṛtāni*) for you—those very ones we have put far away (आरे चक्रम (*āre cakrama*), "we have put them at a distance") out of fear of Indra. Therefore make us, who are not offenders, happy: मृळत (*mṛḷata*) is मृळयत (*mṛḷayata*), सुखयत (*sukhayata*).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हे मरुतः (*he marutaḥ*)—O Maruts
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- आस्मात् (*āsmāt*)—from this famous (and)
- तविषात् (*taviṣāt*)—mighty
- इंद्रात् (*iṃdrāt*)—Indra (from the presence of Indra)
- भिया (*bhiyā*)—through fear
- ईषमाणः (*īṣamāṇaḥ*)—running (unable to stand before him)
- रेजमानः (*rejamānaḥ*)—trembling (I am)
- हव्या (*havyā*)—the oblations (which)
- युष्मभ्यं (*yuṣmabhyaṃ*)—for you
- नितितानि (*nititāni*)—prepared
- आसन् (*āsan*)—were
- तानि (*tāni*)—those
- आरे (*āre*)—at a distance
- चक्रम (*cakrama*)—we have placed (through fear of Indra)
- नः (*naḥ*)—us (who are not offenders)
- मृळत (*mṛḷata*)—make happy

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Maruts, through fear of that violent Indra, I fly, trembling, (from his presence) : the oblations that had been prepared for you have been put away : nevertheless, have patience with us.

### Printed p. 210

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Maruts, through fear of this mighty Indra (unable to stand before him) I run, trembling; through fear of him alone I have put far away the oblation that had been made ready for you. Therefore, make me, who am not an offender, happy.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this verse it is told that the seer Agastya, frightened by Indra, tries to remove his fear and prays to the Maruts. This situation is problematic. The companionship and friendship between Indra and the Maruts is well known from the Veda. Yet Indra, displaying his valour together with the Maruts, states plainly that he got no help at all from them and that he was not benefited by them. On that occasion, since he was angry, the seer Agastya, who had been offering the oblation to the Maruts, trembles with fear. Here he indicates that fear. In the present case, what is the nature of the friendship between Indra and the Maruts, and what is the cause of this hostility in spite of it? This must be examined.

The permanent companionship of Indra and the Maruts, the relation of king and subjects between them, the offering of the oblation in the principal sacrificial rites to Indra accompanied by the Maruts, and the help the Maruts gave Indra when he fought the Asura Vṛtra, are praised in many places in the Saṃhitās and the Brāhmaṇas. A previous story telling how Indra, joining the Maruts, stood apart from the other gods is told in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa:
अतिथ्येन वै देवा इष्ट्वा । तान्त्समदविंदत्ते चतुर्धाव्यद्रवन्नन्योन्यस्य श्रिया तिष्ठमाना अग्निर्वसुभिः सोमो रुद्रैर्वरुण आदित्यैरिंम्रो मरुद्भिर्बृहस्पतिर्विश्वेदेवै ॥ (*atithyena vai devā iṣṭvā | tāntsamadaviṃdatte caturdhāvyadravannanyonyasya śriyā tiṣṭhamānā agnirvasubhiḥ somo rudrairvaruṇa ādityairiṃmro marudbhirbṛhaspatirviśvedevai ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 3-4-2-1).
Formerly, when the gods performed the rite of hospitality, discord arose among them, and, each unwilling to be defeated by the other, they formed four groups and separated. Agni went with the Vasus, Soma with the Rudras, Varuṇa with the Ādityas, Indra with the Maruts, and Bṛhaspati

### Printed p. 211
…formed a separate group together with the Viśvedevas. In this story the close relationship that Indra maintains with the Maruts, through his companionship with them, is stated. Moreover, in many places it is said that Indra is the king and the Maruts are the subjects. For example, in the same Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa:

क्षत्रं ना इंद्रो विशो मरुतो विशा नै क्षत्रियो बलवान् भवति । (*kṣatraṃ nā iṃdro viśo maruto viśā nai kṣatriyo balavān bhavati |*) (Śa. Brā. 4-3-3-6)

Indra is the king, endowed with kṣatriya splendour; the Maruts are the subjects. This indicates that a king becomes powerful only through the cooperation of his subjects, and that Indra's valour grew through the help of the Maruts. Because such a close relationship and companionship exists, the Śrutis show the practice of offering oblations addressed to Indra and the Maruts together. And in Ṛks such as

स नो मरुद्भिवृषभ श्रवो धा उग्र उग्रेभिः स्थविरः सहोदाः (*sa no marudbhivṛṣabha śravo dhā ugra ugrebhiḥ sthaviraḥ sahodāḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-171-5)

Indra and the Maruts are invoked together and an oblation is offered to them together. Describing this companionship, the Bṛhaddevatā explains the epithet that denotes the relation of these deities by sentences such as

मरुद्भिः सह यत्रेंद्रो मरुत्वांस्तत्र सोऽभवत् । (*marudbhiḥ saha yatreṃdro marutvāṃstatra so 'bhavat |*) (Bṛ. Pē. 4-56)**NOTE**

But in the present sūkta, as the result of a dispute between Indra and the Maruts, their mutual enmity and Indra's anger are indicated. The dispute between them, which was the root of this anger, was stated earlier in this way:

क्वंस्यावो मरुतः स्वधासीद्यन्मामेकं समधत्ताहिहत्ये । अहं ह्युंग्रस्तविषस्तुविष्मान्विश्वस्य शत्रोरनमं वधस्नैः ॥ (*kvaṃsyāvo marutaḥ svadhāsīdyanmāmekaṃ samadhattāhihatye | ahaṃ hyuṃgrastaviṣastuviṣmānviśvasya śatroranamaṃ vadhasnaiḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-165-6)

"O Maruts, it is false to say that I killed Ahi with your help. Where indeed was that power of yours? Because my own power is extraordinary, it helped me at the time of killing Vṛtra without seeking the help of any other power. I alone am terrible, of wonderful power and endowed with greatness." Looking on the Maruts with contempt, he thus declares his anger. Here that anger is indicated. It is well known that Indra received the help of the Maruts at the time of slaying the demon Vṛtra. Yet when the Maruts mention that fact and ask that, in memory of that help, they too be given a share in the oblation that belongs to Indra, Indra declares that no help at all came from the Maruts, and that he killed all his enemies by his own power.

### Printed p. 212
The author of the Nirukta, indicating this same matter, says:

अगस्त्य इंद्राय हविर्निरुप्य मरुद्भ्यः संप्रदित्सांचकार । स इंद्र एत्य परिदेवयांचक्रे । (*agastya iṃdrāya havirnirupya marudbhyaḥ saṃpraditsāṃcakāra | sa iṃdra etya paridevayāṃcakre |*) (Ni. 1-5)

Having stated the occasion, namely that when the sage Agastya prepared an oblation for Indra and wished to offer a share of it to the Maruts too, Indra at once objected in the matter, the author of the Nirukta quotes the Ṛk that conveys this point:

न नूनमस्ति नो श्वः कस्तद्वेद यदद्भुतं । अन्यस्य चित्तमभि संचरेण्यमुताधीतं विनश्यति ॥ (*na nūnamasti no śvaḥ kastadveda yadadbhutaṃ | anyasya cittamabhi saṃcareṇyamutādhītaṃ vinaśyati ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-170-1)

Here prayer is addressed to the Maruts, so that the force of that anger may abate and not harm him, and with the purpose that the share of the oblation rightly due to the Maruts may reach them. After such words of conciliation, Indra's friendship grew as before, and he himself gives his share of the oblation to the Maruts. The seer in the eighth Maṇḍala praises this same matter, saying:

वृत्रस्य त्वा त्वसथादीषमाणा विश्वे देवा अजहुर्ये सखायः । (*vṛtrasya tvā tvasathādīṣamāṇā viśve devā ajahurye sakhāyaḥ |*)
मरुद्भरिंद्र सख्यं ते अस्त्वथेमा विश्वाः पृतना जयासि ॥ (*marudbhariṃdra sakhyaṃ te astvathemā viśvāḥ pṛtanā jayāsi ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-96-7)
