Sāyaṇa has used this word as an epithet of the word गावः (*gāvaḥ*) and explained it as "(cows) that yield milk abundantly".

The word गौः (*gauḥ*) is read by the author of the Nirukta among the names of speech (Ni. 2-23). It is also said among the names of earth: गौरिति पृथिव्या नामधेयं (*gauriti pṛthivyā nāmadheyaṃ*) (Ni. 2-5). In गोभिस्सन्नद्दो आसि वीळयस्व (*gobhissannaddo āsi vīḷayasva*), चर्म च श्लेष्मा च गोशब्देनोच्यतॆ (*carma ca śleṣmā ca gośabdenocyatè*): hide and phlegm are denoted by the word गो (*go*). ज्यापि गौरुच्यते आदित्योऽपि गौरुच्यते, सुषुण्णो रश्मिरेक एव गौरिति उच्यते, सर्वॆऽपि रश्मयो गाव उच्यंते (*jyāpi gaurucyate ādityo 'pi gaurucyate, suṣuṇṇo raśmireka eva gauriti ucyate, sarvè 'pi raśmayo gāva ucyaṃte*) (Ni. 2-8): thus for the word गो (*go*) many meanings are stated: hide, phlegm, the rope tied to a bow, the Sun, the Suṣumṇā channel.

Sāyaṇa has explained the word धेनु (*dhenu*) as cows, and has described the sense: बर्हिषि सद्मानं दिव्यं अविनार्सा बर्हिषि जीवंतं दिविभवं इंद्रं त्वां आगत्य परिचरंति क्षीरं दुहंति तथा अर्चाम (*barhiṣi sadmānaṃ divyaṃ avinārsā barhiṣi jīvaṃtaṃ divibhavaṃ iṃdraṃ tvāṃ āgatya paricaraṃti kṣīraṃ duhaṃti tathā arcāma*)—as cows, with their milk, serve Indra, the dweller in heaven who sits comfortably on the barhis, so we, the sacrificers, will worship Indra.

स्वर्वत् (*svarvat*)—फलभूतेन स्वर्गेण तद्वच्छस्त्रं (*phalabhūtena svargeṇa tadvacchastraṃ*): a Śastra that is a means to the fruit of heaven. The word स्वर (*svara*) in the form of an affix-ending is used in hundreds of places in the Ṛgveda. The bare word स्वर (*svara*) too is well known. But its meaning cannot be known exactly. In one place

### Printed p. 247

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

…each has its own meaning. Even so, the meaning must be fixed according to the context. Here the meaning of the basic form of the word स्वर् (*svar*) is given first, and then the sense of स्वर्वत् (*svarvat*) is explained. Compounds such as स्वर्दृशः (*svardṛśaḥ*) and स्वर्विद् (*svarvid*) will be explained only in their own contexts.

This word is read among the common (sādhāraṇa) names: स्वः (*svaḥ*), पृश्निः (*pṛśniḥ*), नाकः (*nākaḥ*) and so on. साधारणान्युत्तराणि षड् दिवश्चादित्यस्य च (*sādhāraṇānyuttarāṇi ṣaḍ divaścādityasya ca*) (Ni. 2-13). The author of the Nirukta has declared that the six words स्वः (*svaḥ*), पृश्निः (*pṛśniḥ*), नाकः (*nākaḥ*), गौः (*gauḥ*), विष्टपं (*viṣṭapaṃ*), नभः (*nabhaḥ*) are common denotations, both as synonyms of the heavenly world and as referring to the Āditya (sun). Then, explaining which root स्वर् (*svar*) comes from when used as a synonym of Āditya, and how the word is derived:

स्वरादित्यो भवति सु अरणः, सु ईरणः, स्वृतो रसान्, स्वृतो भासं ज्योतिषां । स्वृतो भासेति वा । (*svarādityo bhavati su araṇaḥ, su īraṇaḥ, svṛto rasān, svṛto bhāsaṃ jyotiṣāṃ | svṛto bhāseti vā |*) (Ni. 2-14)

The author of the Nirukta has said that the Āditya has this name either because he is far-travelling; or because he wholly scatters and destroys (the darkness); or because he enters into the sap (rasa) and becomes inward with it; or because he spreads his light in the circle of radiance and becomes one with it; or because he pervades the universe in the form of radiance; and that for this very reason the heavenly world, too, has this name.

स्वरादित्यो भवति स एनानि सारयति (*svarādityo bhavati sa enāni sārayati*) (Ni. 5-4)

The Āditya has the name स्वः (*svaḥ*) also because he sets the days in motion. Where this word is used in the Ṛksaṃhitā, in

प्रत्यज् देवानां विशः प्रत्यज्जु देषि मानुषान् । प्रत्यज्विश्वं स्वर्दृशे ॥ (*pratyaj devānāṃ viśaḥ pratyajju deṣi mānuṣān | pratyajviśvaṃ svardṛśe ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-50-5)

and similar Ṛks it is understood as the heavenly world (svarloka); in

त्मना वहंतो दुरो व्यण्णन्नवंत विश्वे स्व १ निर्दृशीके । (*tmanā vahaṃto duro vyaṇṇannavaṃta viśve sva 1 nirdṛśīke |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-69-10)

and similar places, स्वः (*svaḥ*) is understood as "in the sky, in the atmosphere"; and in

दा नो अग्ने बृहतो दाः सहस्रिणो दुरो न वाजं श्रुत्या अपा वृधि । प्राची द्यावापृथिवी ब्रह्मणा कृधि स्वर्ण शुक्रमुषसो वि विद्युतुः ॥ (*dā no agne bṛhato dāḥ sahasriṇo duro na vājaṃ śrutyā apā vṛdhi | prācī dyāvāpṛthivī brahmaṇā kṛdhi svarṇa śukramuṣaso vi vidyutuḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-2-7)

and similar Ṛks as the Āditya. In the same way, words such as स्वर्वत् (*svarvat*), formed with affixes like वत् (*vat*), must be interpreted according to the context. For example—

### Printed p. 248
या इंद्र भुज आभरः स्वर्ना आसुरेभ्यः । स्तोतारमिन्मघवन्नस्य वर्धय ये च त्वे व्यक्तबर्हिषः ॥ (*yā iṃdra bhuja ābharaḥ svarnā āsurebhyaḥ | stotāraminmaghavannasya vardhaya ye ca tve vyaktabarhiṣaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-97-1)

Here स्वर्वान् (*svarvān*) is "possessing happiness, or possessing heaven." Or else the word स्वः (*svaḥ*) is a synonym of "all": because every class of beings has arisen from himself, he is "possessed of that." Since स्वः (*svaḥ*) can mean happiness, heaven or the whole, स्वर्वान् (*svarvān*) means one who has happiness, one who has heaven, or one who has everything; so when it is an epithet of Indra, all these meanings fit. And—

शं शिशीशा स्वध्वरं सत्यं सत्वानमृत्वियं । उतो नु चिद्य ओहत आंडा शुष्णस्य भेदत्यजैः स्वर्वतीरपो नभंतामन्यके समे ॥ (*śaṃ śiśīśā svadhvaraṃ satyaṃ satvānamṛtviyaṃ | uto nu cidya ohata āṃḍā śuṣṇasya bhedatyajaiḥ svarvatīrapo nabhaṃtāmanyake same ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-40-11)

In places like this, where स्वर्वतीः अपः (*svarvatīḥ apaḥ*) is an epithet of water, it means "divine" waters, that is, water produced in the divine world (the world of heaven). This epithet also describes the qualities of other deities such as Agni. For example—

ज्योतिष्मतीमदितिं धारयत् क्षितिं स्वर्वशीमा सचेते दिवेदिवे जागृवांसा दिवेदिवे । (*jyotiṣmatīmaditiṃ dhārayat kṣitiṃ svarvaśīmā sacete divedive jāgṛvāṃsā divedive |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-136-3)

Here स्वर्वती (*svarvatī*) is an epithet of Aditi; it is explained, as in the Ṛk now under discussion, as "possessing heaven" through the accomplishment of the sacrifice.

आ शे शुष्मो वृषभ एतु पश्चादोत्तरादधरादा पुरस्तात् । आ विश्वतो अभि समेश्वर्वाजांद्र द्युम्नं स्वर्वद्धेह्यस्मे ॥ (*ā śe śuṣmo vṛṣabha etu paścādottarādadharādā purastāt | ā viśvato abhi sameśvarvājāṃdra dyumnaṃ svarvaddhehyasme ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-19-9)

In such Ṛks the meaning "wealth joined with happiness" is given. In the present Ṛk, since it is an epithet of the most excellent Sāman, it can be understood either as "a means to heaven" or as "a means to the happiness of heaven."

##### Grammar

- गायत् — गै शब्दे धातुः । छंदसिलुज् लज् लिटः इति वर्तमानार्थे लज् । प्रथमपुरुषैकवचनांतरूपम् । छांदसत्वादडागमो न भवति ।
- नभन्यम् — नभसि भवः नभन्यः । भवार्थे यत् प्रत्ययः । छांदसत्वात् सकारस्य नकारादेशः । शित्‌स्वरितम् इति स्वरितस्वरो भवति ।

### Printed p. 249
- वेः — विद ज्ञाने धातुः । छांदसत्वाद् वर्तमानार्थे लज् । मध्यमपुरुषैकवचने सिप् इकारलोपः । शपो लुक् । हल् ज्याभ्यो इति सिपो लोपः । सिप् निमित्तं धातोर्लघोपधस्य गुणः । दक्ष (पा. सू. ८-२-७५) इति धातुदकारस्य रुत्वम् । विसर्गः । छांदसत्वादडागमो न भवति । तिजिंत निघातस्वरो भवति ।
- अर्जाम — अर्च पूजायां धातुः । लोट् उत्तमपुरुषबहुवचनांतरूपम् । तिजंतस्य परत्वान्निघातस्वरो न भवति । लसार्वधातुकस्यानुदात्तत्वाद् धातुस्वरो अवशिष्यते ।
- नावृधानम् — वृथु वृद्धौ धातुः । लटः शानचादेशः । बहुलं छंदसि इति शपः श्लु आदेशः । श्लु निमित्तं धातोर्द्वित्वादि । छांदसत्वात् संहितायामभ्यासस्य दीर्घो भवति । चितः (पा. सू. ६-१-१६३) इत्यंतोदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- आदब्धाः — नञ् तत्पुरुषसमासः । तत्पुरुषेतुल्यार्थ (पा. सू. ६-२-२) इति नञ् पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरो भवति ।
- दिव्यम् — दिवि भवं दिव्यम् । दंडादिषु पठितत्वाद् य प्रत्ययः । प्रत्ययस्वरेणांतोदात्तं भवति ।
- विवासान् — विवास इति परिचरणार्थे प्रयुक्तः । लेट् प्रथमपुरुषबहुवचने झेरंतादेशः । इतश्चलोपः (पा. सू. ३-४-९७) इति इकारलोपः । लेटोडाट् (पा. सू. ३-४-९४) इति तस्याडागमः । यद्योगान्निघातस्वरो न भवति । व्यत्ययेनाद्युदात्तस्वरो भवति ।

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अर्च॒द्वृषा॒ वृष॑भिः॒ स्वेदु॑हवैर्मृ॒गो नाश्नो॒ अति॒ यज्जु॑गु॒र्यात् ।  
> प्र मं॑द॒युर्म॒नां गू॑र्त॒ होता॒ भर॑ते॒ मर्यो॑ मिथु॒ना  ॑
> यजत्रः ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 250

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अर्चत् । वृषा । वृषभिः । स्वइदुहन्यैः । मृगः । न । अत्मः । अति । यत् । जुगुर्यात् ।  
> प्र । मंदयुः । मनां । गूर्त । होता । भरते । मर्यः । मिथुना । यजत्रः ॥२॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वृषा हविषां वर्षितायं यजमानः स्वेदुहवैः स्वायत्तेद्धहविष्टैः ।
> यद्वा ! स्वयंप्राप्तेंधकं हव्यं येषां तादृशैः । वृषभिर्वर्षकैर्हविष्पदातृभिरध्वय्यादिभिर्विशिष्टः सन्नर्चत् । अर्चयतींद्रं । यद्वा! वृषा फलस्य वर्षितायमिंद्र उक्तलक्षणैर्वयाद्वर्दिभिरच्यते । यद्यस्मादश्नो व्यापकः पानाद्यर्थं धावन्मगो न हरिणादिरिवायमिंद्रोऽश्नोऽतनशीलः सन्नति जुगुर्यात् ! अत्यर्थमुद्युंक्ते हविर्भक्षणाय । यथा मृगः क्षुत्पिपासार्दितः शीघ्रमुद्युंक्ते तथायमिंड्रोऽपि ! यस्मादेवं तस्मादर्चति ! यद्वा ! आयमिंद्रो यद्यथाश्नो मृग
> इवाति जुगुर्यात् भक्षयेत् तथार्चतीत्यर्थः ! किंच हे गूर्तोद्दूर्णेंद्र
> मनां मननं स्तोत्रमिच्छतां देवानां मंदयुः स्तुतियुक्तो मर्यो होता
> मानुष एतन्नामक ऋत्विग्यजत्रो यागनिष्पादकः सन्मिथुना मिथुनानि युग्मरूपाणि
> याज्यापुरोनुवाक्यादीनि मिथुनौ ना जायापतिरूपः सन् प्र भरते । प्रकर्षेण
> संपादयति ! तस्मादर्चतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"Showerer" (वृषा (*vṛṣā*)) means this sacrificer, the one who showers oblations. He worships Indra, accompanied by those who have स्वेदुहवैः (*sveduhavaiḥ*): oblations that are in their own control and kindled, or else those for whom the fuel and oblation have come of themselves. These (वृषभिः (*vṛṣabhiḥ*)) are the "showerers", the givers of oblations, the Adhvaryu and others. Or else: वृषा (*vṛṣā*) is Indra, the showerer of fruit; he is worshipped by those described, the Adhvaryus and the rest.

"As" (यत् (*yat*)): this Indra is *aśnaḥ*, pervading, running for drink and the like, like a deer (मृगः (*mṛgaḥ*)), an antelope and the like; being of an outspreading nature he strives exceedingly (अति जुगुर्यात् (*ati juguryāt*)) to eat the oblation. As a deer tormented by hunger and thirst hastens quickly, so does this Indra. Since this is so, (the sacrificer) worships. Or else: as a pervading deer would devour, so Indra devours; hence the sense "he worships."

Further, O Gūrta, O exalted Indra (so he glosses the vocative): मनां (*manāṃ*) is "thought, praise" — of the gods who desire praise. The human invoker (hotṛ), a priest of that name, मंदयुः (*maṃdayuḥ*), devoted to praise, यजत्रः (*yajatraḥ*), accomplishing the sacrifice, being in the form of a husband-and-wife pair (मिथुनौ ना (*mithunau nā*)), प्र भरते (*pra bharate*): he brings forth abundantly the paired yājyā and puronuvākyā verses; therefore (the sacrificer) worships — such is the sense.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—for what reason
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- अश्वः (*aśvaḥ*)—pervading (running for drink and the like)
- मृगः न (*mṛgaḥ na*)—like an antelope
- अश्वः (*aśvaḥ*)—being of an eating nature
- अति जुगुर्यात् (*ati juguryāt*)—strives exceedingly (to eat the oblation)
- तत् (*tat*)—therefore
- [Or else: अश्वः (*aśvaḥ*)—running for drink and the like; मृगः न (*mṛgaḥ na*)—like a deer; यत् (*yat*)—as; अति जुगुर्यात् (*ati juguryāt*)—(Indra) would strive quickly; (so, in that way)]
- वृषा (*vṛṣā*)—the sacrificer, the showerer of oblations
- स्वेदुहव्यैः (*sveduhavyaiḥ*)—those who have oblations in their own control
- वृषभिः (*vṛṣabhiḥ*)—accompanied by the givers of oblations, the Adhvaryu and others
- अर्चत् (*arcat*)—worships (Indra)
- [Or else: वृषा (*vṛṣā*)—Indra, the showerer of desires; स्वेषुहव्यैः (*sveṣuhavyaiḥ*)—by those who have oblations in their own control; वृषभिः (*vṛṣabhiḥ*)—by the givers of oblations, the Adhvaryu and others; अर्चत् (*arcat*)—is worshipped]
- किंच (*kiṃca*)—and further
- गूर्त (*gūrta*)—O mighty Indra
- मनां (*manāṃ*)—of the gods fond of praise
- मंदयुः (*maṃdayuḥ*)—devoted to praise
- मर्यः (*maryaḥ*)—mortal

### Printed p. 251
- होता (*hotā*)—the hotṛ
- यजत्रः (*yajatraḥ*)—as accomplisher of the sacrifice
- मिथुना (*mithunā*)—the paired yājyā and puronuvākyā verses (in the form of husband and wife)
- प्र भरते (*pra bharate*)—brings together abundantly

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O mighty Indra, the sacrificer, the giver of oblations, accompanied by priests, the Adhvaryu and others, worships you; to receive those oblations you too come quickly, as a deer tormented by hunger and thirst comes running for water and the like. The mortal hotṛ, praising the gods who love praise, recites the yājyā, puronuvākyā and other verses.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
The showerer (of the oblation, the offerer of the sacrifice, assisted) by (the priests), presenting the oblations which have been provided by himself, adores (Indra), in order that he may hasten (to the rite), as a (thirsty) deer to the water. The mortal invoker (of the gods), O mighty Indra, whilst glorifying them who are desirous of praise, presents a twofold offering (to you).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

वृषा स्वेदुहवैः वृषभिरर्चत्— हविषां वर्षिता आयं यजमानः । स्वायत्तेद्वहविष्कैः यद्वा स्वयं प्राप्तेंधकं हव्यं येषां तादृश्यैः हविष्ट्रदातृभिरध्वर्यादिभिः विशिष्टर्स्स अर्चयतींद्रं। (*vṛṣā sveduhavaiḥ vṛṣabhirarcat— haviṣāṃ varṣitā āyaṃ yajamānaḥ | svāyattedvahaviṣkaiḥ yadvā svayaṃ prāpteṃdhakaṃ havyaṃ yeṣāṃ tādṛśyaiḥ haviṣṭradātṛbhiradhvaryādibhiḥ viśiṣṭarssa arcayatīṃdraṃ|*). Sāyaṇa has explained this sentence in two ways. The word वृषा (*vṛṣā*) is used in the sense of the sacrificer who offers the oblations. The word स्वेदुहवैः (*sveduhavaiḥ*) is explained as used as an epithet of the fires and also as an epithet of the priests, the Adhvaryu and others. By this, the first meaning is described: the sacrificer, offering oblations with the deities in view, worships Indra with fire (fires) kindled by fuel (sticks of wood) and by the oblations. In the second meaning the sacrificer, together with the priests, Adhvaryu and others, who stand ready to offer plentiful prepared oblations, worships Indra with abundant oblations. यद्वा वृषा फलस्य वर्षिता इंद्रः उक्तलक्षणैरध्वर्यादिभिरच्यते (*yadvā vṛṣā phalasya varṣitā iṃdraḥ uktalakṣaṇairadhvaryādibhiracyate*): the meaning is also given that Indra, who showers the desired objects on the sacrificers as one sends down rain, is worshipped by the Adhvaryu and other priests, who have prepared plentiful oblations.

### Printed p. 252
Here the two words वृषा (*vṛṣā*) and वृषभिः (*vṛṣabhiḥ*) are both produced from the root वृष (*vṛṣa*). Of the two roots वृषु-सेचने (*vṛṣu-secane*) and वृष-शक्तिबंधने (*vṛṣa-śaktibaṃdhane*), the first word वृषा (*vṛṣā*) is formed from the root वृष (*vṛṣa*) that gives the sense "pour, sprinkle", and the second word वृषभिः (*vṛṣabhiḥ*) from the second root, which gives the sense of power, capacity; this is to be understood from the explanation the commentator makes in this context. But since the epithet वृषन् (*vṛṣan*) and the other epithets derived from this root are used again and again in the Ṛksaṃhitā, one fixed meaning cannot be stated for these words; the meaning has to be given according to each context. Therefore, having first set out in brief the main senses in which the word वृषन् (*vṛṣan*) is used, let us then examine its special meaning where, in the present Ṛk, it is used as an epithet of Indra, or of the priests, or of other deities.

The word वृषन् (*vṛṣan*) is used in many places in the sense of "a male (pumān) with the power of sprinkling"; the word is used either as a synonym of man (puruṣa), or as a synonym of horses and other animals, indicating the masculine.

ये त इंद्र ददुषो वर्धयंति महि क्षत्रं स्थविरं वृष्ट्यं च । (*ye ta iṃdra daduṣo vardhayaṃti mahi kṣatraṃ sthaviraṃ vṛṣṭyaṃ ca |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-54-8).

Here the word वृष्ट्यं (*vṛṣṭyaṃ*) is explained as वृषत्वं (*vṛṣatvaṃ*), पुंत्वं (*puṃtvaṃ*) (virility). And in

स्तुतः श्रवस्यन्न वसोप मद्रिग्युत्वा हरी वृषणा याह्यर्वाज् (*stutaḥ śravasyanna vasopa madrigyutvā harī vṛṣaṇā yāhyarvāj*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-177-1)

here व्यसना (*vyasanā*) is explained as युवासा (*yuvāsā*) (the two young ones). In

भूषन्न योधि बभ्रूषु नम्मते वृषेव पत्नीरभ्येति रोरुवत् । (*bhūṣanna yodhi babhrūṣu nammate vṛṣeva patnīrabhyeti roruvat |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-140-6)

वृषा इव (*vṛṣā iva*) is explained as सेक्ता वृषभ इव (*sektā vṛṣabha iva*), like a bull (ox) that sprinkles. In Ṛks such as

मिनाति श्रियं जरिमा तनूनामप्यू नु पत्नीर्वृषणो जगम्युः । (*mināti śriyaṃ jarimā tanūnāmapyū nu patnīrvṛṣaṇo jagamyuḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-179-1)

वृषणः (*vṛṣaṇaḥ*) is explained as सेक्तारः पुरुषाः (*sektāraḥ puruṣāḥ*), and in many other places likewise the word is used in senses that indicate the masculine and show the power of the male creature. And further—

### Printed p. 253
In verses such as वृषा वां मेघो वृषणा पीपाय गोर्न सेके मनुषो दशस्यन् । (*vṛṣā vāṃ megho vṛṣaṇā pīpāya gorna seke manuṣo daśasyan |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-181-8), दस्मो हि ष्मा वृषणं पिन्वसि त्वचं कं चिद्यावीरररुं शूर मर्त्यं (*dasmo hi ṣmā vṛṣaṇaṃ pinvasi tvacaṃ kaṃ cidyāvīrararuṃ śūra martyaṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-129-3) and ता तू ते सत्या तुविन्नम्म विश्वा प्र धेनवः सिस्रते वृष्ण ऊध्नः । (*tā tū te satyā tuvinnamma viśvā pra dhenavaḥ sisrate vṛṣṇa ūdhnaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-22-6), the word is used in the sense of sprinkling: "one who rains", "one who showers the desired objects", that is, वर्षकः, वर्षणपरमनस्कः (*varṣakaḥ, varṣaṇaparamanaskaḥ*). Other meanings do appear here and there, but throughout, the principal sense of the word वृष्ण (*vṛṣṇa*) is extraordinary strength and virility.

In many verses, such as आ ह्यायस्व समेतु ते विश्वतः सोम वृष्ट्यं (*ā hyāyasva sametu te viśvataḥ soma vṛṣṭyaṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1. 91. 16), महा असि महिष वृष्ट्येभिर्धनस्पृदुग्र सहमानो अन्यान् (*mahā asi mahiṣa vṛṣṭyebhirdhanaspṛdugra sahamāno anyān*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3. 46. 2) and वि चर्मणेव धिषणे अवर्तयद्वैश्वानरो विश्वमधत्त वृष्ट्यं (*vi carmaṇeva dhiṣaṇe avartayadvaiśvānaro viśvamadhatta vṛṣṭyaṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6. 8. 3), the word वृष्ण (*vṛṣṇa*) commonly means virility and valour. Further, since the word वृषन् (*vṛṣan*) is in many contexts used as a synonym of Indra and other deities, in such places one must infer from the context which deity is meant.

In अपोषा अनसः सरत्संपिष्टादह बिभ्युषी । नि यत्सीं शिश्नथद्वृषा (*apoṣā anasaḥ saratsaṃpiṣṭādaha bibhyuṣī | ni yatsīṃ śiśnathadvṛṣā*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-30-10) one may infer that it is a synonym of Indra. But in many places it raises doubt as to which deity is meant. Sometimes even Sāyaṇācārya shows doubt in the present verse, and his explanation when interpreting this epithet is not so precise.

In वृषा छंदुर्भवति हठ्यतो वृषा क्षेमेण थेसां मघवा यदिन्वति (*vṛṣā chaṃdurbhavati haṭhyato vṛṣā kṣemeṇa thesāṃ maghavā yadinvati*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-55-4)
### Printed p. 254
and in the present verse, as he explains the word वृषा (*vṛṣā*) he first says वृषाहविषां वर्षयिता यजमानः (*vṛṣāhaviṣāṃ varṣayitā yajamānaḥ*) and then यद्वा वृषा-इंद्रः (*yadvā vṛṣā-iṃdraḥ*), thus giving room for doubt. Since the word is used in many senses, in each context one must understand the sense suited to it. In explaining this word, full of nuance, the English commentator Max Muller describes the depth of the ideas expressed by it as follows.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
In वृषन् (*vṛṣan*) we have one of those words which it is almost impossible to transalate accurately. It oceurs over and over again in the Vedic hymns and if we once know the various ideas which it either expresses or implies we have little difficulty in understanding its import in a vague and general way though we look in vain for corresponding terms in any modern language and we shall see how difficult it would be to translate such terms without losing either the key-note that was still sounding, or the harmonies which were set vibrating by it in the minds of the poets and their listeners [S B.E. vol. XXXII, P. 138-139]

In the present verse it is understood either of the officiating priests who bear the oblations, or of Indra and others, who are endowed with virility and shower the desired objects.

अश्वः मृगो न अतिजुगुर्यात्—व्यापकः पानाद्यर्थं धावन् हरिणादिरिव अयमिंद्रः अशनशीलस्सर् अत्यर्थमुद्युज्यो हविर्भक्षणाय (*aśvaḥ mṛgo na atijuguryāt—vyāpakaḥ pānādyarthaṃ dhāvan hariṇādiriva ayamiṃdraḥ aśanaśīlassar atyarthamudyujyo havirbhakṣaṇāya*). The word अश्वः (*aśvaḥ*) is stated among the names of cloud (Ni. 2-21). Here, as व्यापकः (*vyāpakaḥ*) it refers to the deer, and as अशनशीलः (*aśanaśīlaḥ*) to Indra, both senses being conveyed. Since the word न (*na*) is used with a twofold construction, one meaning is described by comparison with a deer and similar animals: like a deer running to drink water and the like, Indra comes quickly and is eager to accept our oblations.

मृगः (*mṛgaḥ*)—मृगो मार्ष्टेर्गतिकर्मणः (*mṛgo mārṣṭergatikarmaṇaḥ*) (Ni. 1-20): formed from the root मृज् (*mṛj*) meaning motion, and with नित्यं ह्यसौ गच्छति (*nityaṃ hyasau gacchati*) it gives the sense "one who is always moving". अन्वेषणशीलो ना (*anveṣaṇaśīlo nā*): the purport is also "one who is always searching for his food".

यथा क्षुत्पिपासार्दितः मृगः शीघ्रमुद्युज्ज्वले तधायमिंद्रोऽपि (*yathā kṣutpipāsārditaḥ mṛgaḥ śīghramudyujjvale tadhāyamiṃdro 'pi*): as a deer afflicted by hunger and thirst runs quickly for its food and drink, so Indra comes quickly to accept the oblation; this is the second meaning described.
### Printed p. 255
यद्वा अयमिंद्रः अश्नो मृग इव अतिजुगुर्यात् भक्षयेत् तथा अर्चति (*yadvā ayamiṃdraḥ aśno mṛga iva atijuguryāt bhakṣayet tathā arcati*). A deer wearied by hunger and thirst eats abundantly, destroys its hunger and is satisfied; likewise the sacrificer worships Indra so that his hunger subsides and he is satisfied. This meaning is also described.

जुगुर्यात् (*juguryāt*)—जुगुर्यात्, जुगुरुः जुगुर्वणिः (*juguryāt, juguruḥ jugurvaṇiḥ*) and such words are all to be understood as formed from the root जृ (*jṛ*). The Nirukta authors included the word जठे (*jaṭhe*) among the names of the act of worship (अर्चति (*arcati*)). It is used often with the prefixes अभि (*abhi*), अति (*ati*) and so on. For example, in अभी नो अग्न उक्कमिज्जुगुर्या द्यावाक्षामा सिंधवश्च स्वगूर्ताः (*abhī no agna ukkamijjuguryā dyāvākṣāmā siṃdhavaśca svagūrtāḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-140-13), अभिमुख्येन स्तुहि (*abhimukhyena stuhi*) means: pronounce the word of assent, "I have praised in a special manner".

In verses such as प्रस्तोषदुप गासिषच्छ्रवत्साम गीयमानं । अभि राधसा जुगुरत् (*prastoṣadupa gāsiṣacchravatsāma gīyamānaṃ | abhi rādhasā jugurat*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-81-5), अभिगृणातु (*abhigṛṇātu*) means "let him accept". That is, the word is understood to be used where a special degree of respect is shown, whether in the act of worship or in acceptance. In the present verse too it is used in the sense of respectful acceptance.

मनां मंडयुः मर्यः होता यजत्रः (*manāṃ maṃḍayuḥ maryaḥ hotā yajatraḥ*): addressing Indra, one meaning is described thus: the Hotṛ priest, a human being who recites the praises of the gods who desire to hear the hymn, is the performer of the sacrifice; and this Hotṛ recites together the mantras called Yājyā and Purone-vākyā. In the second meaning, this priest called Hotṛ, being in the form of a couple (husband and wife), performs the sacrifice without interruption, and therefore the sacrificer worships Indra; this is the meaning described.

गूर्त (*gūrta*)—Here the word गूर्त (*gūrta*) means "powerful". It also means "praised", "worthy of praise".

In शं वः सखायो मदाय पुनानमभि गायत । शिशुं न यज्ञैः स्वदयंत गूर्तिभिः (*śaṃ vaḥ sakhāyo madāya punānamabhi gāyata | śiśuṃ na yajñaiḥ svadayaṃta gūrtibhiḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-105-1), गूर्तिभिः (*gūrtibhiḥ*) means स्तुतिभिः (*stutibhiḥ*), "by hymns"; and in
तं गूर्तयो नेमन्निषः परीणसः समुद्रं न संचरणे सनिष्यवः । पतिं दक्षस्य विदथस्य नू सहो गिरिं न वेना अधि रोह तेजसा ॥ (*taṃ gūrtayo nemanniṣaḥ parīṇasaḥ samudraṃ na saṃcaraṇe saniṣyavaḥ | patiṃ dakṣasya vidathasya nū saho giriṃ na venā adhi roha tejasā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-56-2)
### Printed p. 256
and similar places it also means स्तोकारः (*stokāraḥ*). In the present verse it means the praiseworthy valour of Indra.

मर्यः (*maryaḥ*)—मर्यो मनुष्यो मरणधर्मा (*maryo manuṣyo maraṇadharmā*). Because he is subject to death, man is called मर्य (*marya*) (Ni. 3-15).

##### Grammar

- अर्जिश्—अर्च पूजायां शातु । छांदसवागि लज् ।
- स्वेदु हव्यैः—स्वयं इत् प्राप्तं हव्यं येषां ते स्वे दुहव्याः । तैः । बहुव्रीह् प्रकृत्या पूर्वपदम् इति पूर्वपद प्रकृतिस्वरो भवति ।
- जुगुर्यात्—गृ शब्दे धातुः । लिजि बहुलं छंदसि इति विकरणस्य श्लु आदेशः । बहुलं छंदसि इति धातोरुत्वं रपरत्वं च भवति । श्लुनिमित्तं धातोर्द्वित्वम् । कुहोश्चुः (पा. सू. ७-४-६२) इति चुत्वम् । यद्योगान्निघातस्वरो न भवति । यासुट् उदात्त इति अंतोदात्तं भवति ।
- मंदयुः—मंदमात्मनः इच्छति । सुप आत्मनः क्यच् (पा. सू. ३-१-८) इति क्यच् । क्याच्छंदसि (पा. सू. ३-२-१७०) इति उ प्रत्ययः । अशो लोपः (पा. सू. ६-४-४८) इति क्यचोऽकारलोपः । प्रत्ययस्वरो अवशिष्यते ।
- मनाम्—मन ज्ञाने धातुः । कर्तरि क्विप् प्रत्ययः । षष्ठीबहुवचनांतरूपम् । सावेकाचस्तृतीयादिः (पा. सू. ६-१-१६) इति विभक्तेरुदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- भरते—भृञ् भरणे धातुः । लट् प्रथमपुरुषैकवचनांतरूपम् । पादादित्वान्निघातस्वरो न भवति । लः सार्वधातुकः । शप् अनुदात्तत्वाद्धातुस्वरो अवशिष्यते ।
- मर्यः—मृज् प्राणत्यागे धातुः अंतर्भावितण्यर्थकः । छंदसि निष्कर्ष्य- (पा. सू. ३-१-१२३) इति निपातनाद्यत् प्रत्ययः । बहुलग्रहणात् कर्तरि भवति । यतोनावः (पा. सू ६-१-२१३) इति सूत्राद् आद्युदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- मिथुना—मिथ इति सौत्रो धातुः । क्षुधिपिशि—(उ. सू ३३५) इति उनन् प्रत्ययः । कित् भवति । अत एव लघूपधगुणो न भवति । नपुंसके शेच्छंदसि बहुलम् इति शेर्लोपः ।
- यजत्रः—यज देवपूजासंगतिकरणदानेषु धातुः । अमिनक्षि यजिवधि— (उ. सू. ३८५) इति अत्रस् प्रत्ययः । नित्त्वाद् आद्युदात्तस्वरो भवति ।

### Printed p. 257
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> नक्ष॒द्दोता॒ परि॒ सद्म॑ मि॒ता यन्भर॒द्गर्भ॒मा श॒रदः॑ पृथि॒व्याः ।  
> क्रंद॒दश्वो॒ नय॑मानो रु॒वद्गौरं॒तर्दू॒तो न रोद॑सी  ॒
> चरद्वाक् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नक्षत् । होता । परि । सद्म । मिता । यन् । भरत् । गर्भं । आ । शरदः । पृथिव्याः ।  
> क्रंदत् । अश्वः । नयमानः । रुवत् । गौः । अंतः । दूतः । न । रोदसी इति । चरत् ।  
> वाक् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> होता होमनिष्पादकोऽयमुग्निर्मिता परिमिता सद्म सदनानि गार्हपत्यादिस्थानानि परि यन् परितो गच्छन्नक्षत् । व्याप्ोति । व्याप्य च शरदः संवत्सरसंबंधिनं
> पृथिव्या भूम्याश्च संबंधिनं गर्भं गर्भस्थानीयमन्नं हविर्लक्षणमा भरत् । आहरति ।
> स्वीकरोति । संभरति वा। संवत्सरपर्यंतं. वर्धितं भूम्यामुत्पन्नमित्यर्थः । यद्वा।
> संवत्सरसंबंधिनं वसंतादिकाले ज्योतिष्टोमादिषु दीयमानं पार्थिवं हविर्भरत् । वसंते वसंते ज्योतिषा यजेतेति श्रुतेः । तथा कृत्वा क्रंददत्वः । लुप्तोपममेतत् । अश्व इव क्रंदति हर्षातिशयात् । किं कुर्वन् । नयमानो नयदिंद्राय हविर्भारं । किंच गौर्गंतोद्वृत्तो वृष इव रुवत् । रौति । शब्दायते ।
> किं कुर्वन् । दूतो न वार्ताहरो दूत इव रोदसी रोदस्योर्द्यावापृथिव्योरंतर्मध्यॆ रोदस्कोरंतरा-वाक् स्तूयमानः स्तुवन् वा देवांश्चरत् । चरति ।
> गच्छति । हविःस्वीकाराय पृथिवीं तत्पापणाय द्युलोकं च परिभ्रमतीत्यर्थः ॥
> वक्तेः कर्तरि कर्मणि वा क्विब्बचीत्यादिना क्विब्बिर्घौ संप्रसारणाभावश्च ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

होता (*hotā*) is this Agni, who accomplishes the offering. Going around (परि यन् (*pari yan*)) the measured stations (सद्म (*sadma*)), the Gārhapatya and the other hearths, he pervades them (नक्षत् (*nakṣat*) = व्याप्ोति (*vyāpoti*)). Having pervaded them, he takes up (आ भरत् (*ā bharat*) = आहरति (*āharati*), accepts, or collects) the "germ" (गर्भं (*garbhaṃ*)), the food in the form of oblation, which stands in the place of a germ, belonging to the year (शरदः (*śaradaḥ*)) and to the earth (पृथिव्याः (*pṛthivyāḥ*)): that is, what has grown for a year and been produced on the earth. Or else: the earthly oblation belonging to the year, given in the spring and other seasons in the Jyotiṣṭoma and other rites, he bears; for the śruti says वसंते वसंते ज्योतिषा यजेत (*vasaṃte vasaṃte jyotiṣā yajeta*). Having done so, he क्रंदत् (*kraṃdat*), "neighs like a horse", the simile being left unexpressed, from excess of joy. Doing what? नयमानः (*nayamānaḥ*), bearing the burden of oblation to Indra. Moreover he रुवत् (*ruvat*) (रौति (*rauti*), makes sound) like a गौः (*gauḥ*), a bull that has gone off, grown wild. Doing what? Like a messenger (दूतो न (*dūto na*)), a bearer of tidings, रोदसी अंतः (*rodasī aṃtaḥ*), between heaven and earth, being praised or praising the gods, he चरत् (*carat*) (चरति (*carati*), goes): he roams the earth to receive the oblation and the heavenly world to convey it. Then the closing note on the word वाक् (*vāk*), formed from वक्तेः (*vakteḥ*) with क्विप् (*kvip*) in the active or passive.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- होता (*hotā*)—Agni, who accomplishes the offering
- मिता (*mitā*)—measured
- सद्म (*sadma*)—the stations, Gārhapatya and the rest
- परि यन् (*pari yan*)—going around on all sides
- नक्षत् (*nakṣat*)—pervades (then)
- शरदः (*śaradaḥ*)—belonging to the year (up to the year's end)
- पृथिव्याः (*pṛthivyāḥ*)—belonging to the earth (being on the earth, of it)
- गर्भं (*garbhaṃ*)—the oblation that stands in the place of the (earth's) germ
- आ भरत् (*ā bharat*)—
### Printed p. 258
- accepts (obtains) it; [or शरदः (*śaradaḥ*)—belonging to the year, in the spring and other seasons; offered in the Jyotiṣṭoma and other rites; पृथिव्याः गर्भं (*pṛthivyāḥ garbhaṃ*)—the oblation that is the earth's germ; आ भरत् (*ā bharat*)—accepts] (having accepted)
- नयमानः (*nayamānaḥ*)—bearing (the oblation for Indra)
- अश्वः क्रंदत् (*aśvaḥ kraṃdat*)—neighs (with joy) like a horse (and)
- दूतः न (*dūtaḥ na*)—like a messenger who brings tidings
- रोदसी अंतः (*rodasī aṃtaḥ*)—between heaven and earth
- वाक् (*vāk*)—being praised (praising the gods)
- चरत् (*carat*)—roaming
- गौः (*gauḥ*)—like a bull
- रुवत् (*ruvat*)—makes sound

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Agni, the accomplisher of the offering, pervades the measured stations, Gārhapatya and the rest, and accepts the food in the form of oblation, offered in the Jyotiṣṭoma and other rites, which stands in the place of the earth's germ. Then, bearing it for Indra and the others, he neighs with joy like a horse; and as a messenger who furnishes news to the gods, roaming between heaven and earth, he bellows like a bull.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
The invoker (of the gods, Agni), pervades the measured stations (of the altars), and accepts that (oblation), which is the germ of the year and of the earth ; as a horse neighs when bringing (the offering to Indra), as a bull bellows like a messenger proclaiming (his message) between and earth.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

होता (*hotā*)—होमनिष्पादको आयमग्निः (*homaniṣpādako āyamagniḥ*). Although the word होतृ (*hotṛ*) denotes the priest who invites the gods to accept the oblation, here the meaning is explained as Agni, who, in order that the oblations offered by the sacrificers reach the gods, brings the gods, offers the oblation to them and carries the sacrifice through. This sense is used especially with regard to Agni.

Further, होमनिष्पादकं आह्वातारं वा (*homaniṣpādakaṃ āhvātāraṃ vā*): the accomplisher of the offering by oblations and other offerings, or the one who gives the invitation to the gods. Thus the meanings of the word होतृ (*hotṛ*) are given, since the Hotṛ has two kinds of function. The same view is explained in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa in the form of a discussion, as follows. It shows the place of the Hotṛ in the sacrifice.

### Printed p. 259

यज्ञो वै देवेभ्य उदक्रामत्तमिष्टिभिः प्रैषमैच्छन्यदिष्टिभिः प्रैषमैच्छंस्तदिष्टीनामिष्टित्वं तमन्विंदन् । अनुवित्तयज्ञो राध्योति य एवं वेद । आहूतयो वै नामैता यदाहुतय एताभिर्वै देवान्यजमानो ह्वयति तदाहुतीनामाहूतित्वं । ऊतयः खलु वै ता नाम याभिर्देना यजमानस्य हवमायंति ये वै पंथानो याः स्रुतयस्टा वा ऊतयस्त उ एवैतत्स्वर्गयाणा यजमानस्य भवंति ॥ (*yajño vai devebhya udakrāmattamiṣṭibhiḥ praiṣamaicchanyadiṣṭibhiḥ praiṣamaicchaṃstadiṣṭīnāmiṣṭitvaṃ tamanviṃdan | anuvittayajño rādhyoti ya evaṃ veda | āhūtayo vai nāmaitā yadāhutaya etābhirvai devānyajamāno hvayati tadāhutīnāmāhūtitvaṃ | ūtayaḥ khalu vai tā nāma yābhirdenā yajamānasya havamāyaṃti ye vai paṃthāno yāḥ srutayasṭā vā ūtayasta u evaitatsvargayāṇā yajamānasya bhavaṃti ‖*) (Ai. Brā. 1-2).

Once the sacrifice went away from the gods. They then recovered it by offering oblations. An āhuti (oblation) is an offering made by calling on the name of a deity. If it is said that the one who offers such oblations is called the Hotṛ, another question arises:

तदाहुर्यदन्यो जुहोत्यथ योऽनु चाह यजति च कस्मात्तं होतेत्याचक्षत इति । यद्वाव स तत्र यथाभाजनं देवता अमुमावहामुमावहेत्यावाहयति तदेव होतुर्होतृत्वं होता भवति । होतेत्येनमाचक्षते य एवं वेद ॥ (*tadāhuryadanyo juhotyatha yo 'nu cāha yajati ca kasmāttaṃ hotetyācakṣata iti | yadvāva sa tatra yathābhājanaṃ devatā amumāvahāmumāvahetyāvāhayati tadeva hoturhotṛtvaṃ hotā bhavati | hotetyenamācakṣate ya evaṃ veda ‖*) (Ai. Brā. 1-2).

Even though the one who makes the offering is one person and the one who gives the invitation to the offering and utters the mantra is another, the question is how the name Hotṛ can be fitting for the one who utters the offering mantra. The answer given is that the reciter of this mantra calls the respective deities in keeping with the measure of each offering, and so the name Hotṛ is right for him also. Then:

मनो नै यज्ञस्य मैत्रावरुणो वाग्यज्ञस्य होता । (*mano nai yajñasya maitrāvaruṇo vāgyajñasya hotā |*) (Ai. Brā. 6-5).

By this sentence the identity of speech, which recites the mantra, with the Hotṛ is stated. And:

सीद होतः स्व उ लोके चिकित्वानित्यग्निर्वै देवानां होता तस्यैष स्वो लोको यदुत्तरवेदीनाभिः.................. (*sīda hotaḥ sva u loke cikitvānityagnirvai devānāṃ hotā tasyaiṣa svo loko yaduttaravedīnābhiḥ..................*) (Ai. Brā. 5-2).

Such sentences address Agni, the accomplisher of the homa, as the Hotṛ of the gods. Therefore in the present Ṛk the word होतारं (*hotāraṃ*) can be explained as होमनिष्पादकं आह्वातारं ना (*homaniṣpādakaṃ āhvātāraṃ nā*). With the words क्रंददश्वः (*kraṃdadaśvaḥ*) and so on, Agni's function as Hotṛ is described.

### Printed p. 260

नक्षत् (*nakṣat*)—व्याप्नोति । (*vyāpnoti |*) (he pervades). Since इन्वति, नक्षति, आक्षाणाः (*invati, nakṣati, ākṣāṇāḥ*) and so on are read among the 10 names of the action of pervading (Ni. 3-10), the word अभिनक्षति (*abhinakṣati*) means: pervade all around, surround.

प्र पर्वता अनवंत प्र गावः प्र ब्रह्माणो अभिनक्षंत इंद्रं । (*pra parvatā anavaṃta pra gāvaḥ pra brahmāṇo abhinakṣaṃta iṃdraṃ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-96-5).

सरस्वतीं यां पितरो हवंते दक्षिणा यज्ञमभिनक्षमाणाः ॥ (*sarasvatīṃ yāṃ pitaro havaṃte dakṣiṇā yajñamabhinakṣamāṇāḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-17-9).

In such Ṛks, अभिनक्षंतः (*abhinakṣaṃtaḥ*) and अभिनक्षमाणाः (*abhinakṣamāṇāḥ*) mean: they stood surrounding. Here the meaning is that the sacrificial fire stands pervading the sacrificial dwelling, the Gārhapatya and the rest.

मिता सद्म परियन् नक्षत् (*mitā sadma pariyan nakṣat*)—the word सद्म (*sadma*) is stated among the names of water (Ni. 2-24), among the names of battle (Ni. 3-9), among the names of house (Ni. 3-13), and as सद्मनी (*sadmanī*) among the names of heaven and earth (Ni. 3-22). Here it is used in the sense of a house, a dwelling-place. The meaning described here is that Agni fully pervades his own dwelling-place, called Āhavanīya and Gārhapatya in accordance with his name, constructed according to the order stated in the śāstra.

शरदः पृथिव्याः गर्भमाभरत् (*śaradaḥ pṛthivyāḥ garbhamābharat*)—संवत्सरसंबंधिनं भूम्याश्च संबंधिनं हविर्लक्षणं गर्भस्थानीयमन्नं स्वीकरोति संभरति वा । (*saṃvatsarasaṃbaṃdhinaṃ bhūmyāśca saṃbaṃdhinaṃ havirlakṣaṇaṃ garbhasthānīyamannaṃ svīkaroti saṃbharati vā |*) The word शरत् (*śarat*) means the autumn season: the time in which the herbs (trees, roots and so on) become dry, शरच्छृता आस्यामोषधयो भवंति (*śaracchṛtā āsyāmoṣadhayo bhavaṃti*); or the time in which waters are dried up, शीर्षा आप इति वा (*śīrṣā āpa iti vā*) (Ni. 4 26). The meaning explained is that Agni makes the food, in the form of oblation, which is produced on earth up to the end of the year and dried up in autumn, to be produced, or he receives it.

In the second meaning, वसंते वसंते ज्योतिषा यजेत (*vasaṃte vasaṃte jyotiṣā yajeta*) इति श्रुतेः । (*iti śruteḥ |*) वसंतादिकाले ज्योतिष्टोमादिषु संवत्सरसंबंधिनं दीप्यमानं पार्थिवं हविः भरत् (*vasaṃtādikāle jyotiṣṭomādiṣu saṃvatsarasaṃbaṃdhinaṃ dīpyamānaṃ pārthivaṃ haviḥ bharat*). Since the śruti says that the Jyotiṣṭoma sacrifice is to be performed in the spring season, the meaning described is that, for the performance of the respective sacrifices such as the Jyotiṣṭoma, Agni receives, in the respective seasons stated in the śāstra, such as spring, the radiant oblations produced on earth until the year is complete.

Further, showing the relation of Agni, spring and oblation, the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa states thus:

तद्यदेतेऽत्रोपदधाति। संवत्सर एषोऽग्निरिमऽउ लोकाः संवत्सरस्त्यस्यायमेव लोकः प्रथमा चितिरयमस्य लोको वसंत ऋतुस्तद्यदेतेऽत्रोपदधाति यदेवास्यैते आत्मनस्तदस्मिन्नेतत्पतिदधाति तस्मादेतेऽत्रोप दधाति ॥ (*tadyadete 'tropadadhāti| saṃvatsara eṣo 'gnirima 'u lokāḥ saṃvatsarastyasyāyameva lokaḥ prathamā citirayamasya loko vasaṃta ṛtustadyadete 'tropadadhāti yadevāsyaite ātmanastadasminnetatpatidadhāti tasmādete 'tropa dadhāti ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 7-4-2-30).

### Printed p. 261

In these sentences the identity of Agni with the year is stated first, and then the identity of the first layer (citi) with spring. And:

यद्वैवैतेऽत्रोपदधाति । प्रजापतिरेषोऽग्निः संवत्सर उ प्रजापतिस्तस्य प्रतिष्ठैव प्रथमा चितिः प्रतिष्ठोऽस्य वसंत ऋतुस्तद्यदेतेऽत्रोपदधाति .........तेऽनंतरहिते विश्वज्योतिष उपदधाति प्रजा वै विश्वज्योतिः........ ... ऋतुभिर्हैव गर्भे संतं संपश्यन्मृतुभिर्जातं ॥ (*yadvaivaite 'tropadadhāti | prajāpatireṣo 'gniḥ saṃvatsara u prajāpatistasya pratiṣṭhaiva prathamā citiḥ pratiṣṭho 'sya vasaṃta ṛtustadyadete 'tropadadhāti .........te 'naṃtarahite viśvajyotiṣa upadadhāti prajā vai viśvajyotiḥ........ ... ṛtubhirhaiva garbhe saṃtaṃ saṃpaśyanmṛtubhirjātaṃ ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 7-4-2-31).

By such sentences the identity of the earth with the year is shown, and it is explained that the oblation and other food produced in the womb of the year is like what is produced in the womb of the earth.

क्रंददश्वः (*kraṃdadaśvaḥ*)—अश्व इव क्रंदति हर्षातिशयात् । (*aśva iva kraṃdati harṣātiśayāt |*) Although the word इव (*iva*), which denotes comparison, is not used in this sentence, it is described that Agni, having received the oblation, makes a sound as a joyful horse neighs.

नयमानः गौः रुवत् (*nayamānaḥ gauḥ ruvat*)—नयन्निंद्राय हनिर्भारं । (*nayanniṃdrāya hanirbhāraṃ |*) It is explained that Agni carries oblations in heaps for Indra. The word गौः (*gauḥ*) is explained as a bull that bears a load, and Agni's sound is compared to that of a rutting bull carrying a burden.

दूतो न रोदसी अंतः वाक् चरन् (*dūto na rodasī aṃtaḥ vāk caran*)—Like a messenger who conveys news to the persons concerned, Agni moves between heaven and earth, either praised by the Ṛtvijs or himself praising the gods; this is explained, and then: हविस्वीकाराय पृथिवीं तत्पाप णाय द्युलोकं च परिभ्रमति (*havisvīkārāya pṛthivīṃ tatpāpa ṇāya dyulokaṃ ca paribhramati*). That is, Agni comes to the earth to receive the oblations and goes to heaven to convey the received oblations to the gods; thus the order of Agni's movement is described. In this movement of Agni the comparison with a news-bearing messenger is expressed.

##### Grammar

- नक्षत् — नक्ष व्याप्तौ धातुः । छांदसं वर्तमानार्थे लज् । प्रथमपुरुषैकवचनांतरूपं । छांदसत्वादडागमो न भवति । पादादित्वान्निघातस्वरो न भवति । लसार्वधातुकस्यानुदात्तत्वाद्धातुस्वरः शिष्यते ।
- सद्म — षड्ल्य विशरणगत्यवसादनेतु धातुः । औणादिको मसिन् प्रत्ययः । नित्त्वादाद्युदात्तो भवति ।

### Printed p. 262

- यस् — इण् गतौ धातुः । लटः शत्रादेशः । इगो यस् (ष. सू. ६-४-८१) इति यणादेशः । प्रत्ययस्याद्युदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- भरत् — हृञ् हरणे धातुः । छांदसं वर्तमानार्थे लज् । प्रथमपुरुषैकवचनांतरूपं । छांदसत्वादडागमो न भवति । हृग्ग्रहोर्भश्चंदसि इति वचनाद्धकारस्य भकारादेशः । पादादित्वान्निघातस्वरो न भवति । शप् पित्त्वाद्धातुस्वरः शिष्यते ।
- क्रंदत् — क्रदि आह्वाने रोदने च धातुः । लटः शत्रादेशः । इदितोनुम् धातोति इति धातोर्नुमागमः । अथवा पूर्ववच्छांदसं लज् । धातुस्वरः शिष्यते ।
- नयमानः — णीञ् प्रापणे धातुः । लटः शानचादेशः । शप् विकरणः । धातोर्गुणोऽयादेशः । आनेमुक् (पा. सू. ७-२-८२) इति मुकागमः । ऐ-सार्वधातुकस्य शास्यनुदात्तेत्—सूत्रेणानुदात्तत्वाद्धातुस्वरः शिष्यते ।
- रुवत् — रु शब्दे धातुः । लटः शत्रादेशः । अचिश्नुधातुभ्रुवां- इत्यनेनोपजादेशः । प्रत्ययस्याद्युदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- चरत् — चर गतिभक्षणयोः धातुः । छंदसं वर्तमानार्थे लज् । प्रथमपुरुषैकवचनांतरूपं । बहुलं छंदस्यमाज्‌योगेऽपि इत्यनेनाडागमो न भवति । निघातस्वरो भवति ।
- वाक् — वच परिभाषणे धातुः । क्विब्वचॆप्रच्छ— (उ. सू. २-२१५) इत्यनेन कर्तरि कर्मणि वा क्विप् प्रत्ययः । दीर्घः । संप्रसारणाभावः । पदांशे कुत्थ ।

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ता क॒र्माष॑तरास्मै॒ प्र च्यौ॒त्नानि॑ देव॒यंतो॑ भरंते ।  
> जुजो॑ष॒दिंद्रो॑ द॒स्यव॑र्चा॒ नास॑त्येव॒ सुग्मो॑ रथे॒ष्णाः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ता । कर्म । अषतरा । आस्मै । प्र । ज्यौत्नानि । देवयंतः । भरंते ।  
> जुजोषत् । इंद्रः । दस्यवर्चाः । नासत्याइव । सुग्म्यः । रथेऽस्थाः ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 263

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ता शास्त्रषतरार्षतराणि व्याप्ततराणि वा हवींष्यस्मा इंद्राय तदर्थं कर्म।
> कुर्मः । संपादयामः। कृइश्कांदसे वर्तमानार्थे लुजि मंत्रे घसेति चेर्लुक्। छंदस्युभयथेत्यार्धधातुकत्वेन जित्वाभानाद्गुणः ॥ च्यौत्नानि च्यावयित्रीणि
> दृढानि स्तोत्राणि कर्माणि स्वकीयानि सामर्थ्य्यानि ना देवयंतो देवानात्मन
> इच्छंतो यजमानाः प्र भरंते । प्रकर्षण संपादयंति । तद्वद्वयमपि संपाद
> याम इत्यर्थः । दस्मवर्चा दर्शनीयतेजाः शत्रुपक्षयणबलो ना नासत्येव
> नासत्याविवाश्विनाविव । तौ हि द्यावापृथिव्यात्मानौ ! ताविव सुग्म्यः सुगम्यः सुखेन
> गंतुं शक्यो रथेष्णा रथे वर्तमान एवंभूतः सङ्जु जोषत् । अस्मत्कृतं
> स्तोत्रादि कर्म सेवतां ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says that those oblations, which are most pervading, we offer to this Indra and for his sake: कर्म (*karma*) means "we do, we accomplish". **NOTE**. च्यौत्नानि (*cyautnāni*) means things that make fall or drive away, firm hymns, deeds, or one's own powers. Worshippers who desire the gods for themselves offer these forth (प्र भरंते (*pra bharaṃte*), "they accomplish in abundance"); in the same way we too accomplish them. दस्मवर्चा (*dasmavarcā*) is one of beautiful radiance, or one whose strength destroys the party of enemies. He is like the Nāsatyas, that is, like the Aśvins, who are of the nature of heaven and earth; like them he is सुग्म्यः (*sugmyaḥ*), easy to approach, one who can be reached with ease, and रथेष्णा (*ratheṣṇā*), standing in his chariot. Being such, may he accept (जुजोषत् (*jujoṣat*)) the hymns and other acts done by us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अषतरा (*aṣatarā*)—flowing abundantly (pervading)
- ता (*tā*)—those oblations
- अस्मै (*asmai*)—for this Indra
- कर्म (*karma*)—let us do (accomplish)
- देवयंतः (*devayaṃtaḥ*)—the worshippers who desire the gods (their favour)
- च्यौत्नानि (*cyautnāni*)—firm acts in the form of praise
- प्र भरंते (*pra bharaṃte*)—(the worshippers) accomplish abundantly
- दस्यवर्चाः (*dasyavarcāḥ*)—he who is endowed with beautiful radiance, or with strength that destroys enemies
- नासत्या इव (*nāsatyā iva*)—like the Aśvin deities
- सुग्म्यः इंद्रः (*sugmyaḥ iṃdraḥ*)—Indra, who is easy to approach
- रथेष्णाः (*ratheṣṇāḥ*)—seated in the chariot
- जुजोषत् (*jujoṣat*)—may he accept (our praises and the like)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Worshippers who long for the favour of the gods perform in abundance such acts as praise. We too, like them, accomplish oblations and the like that are abundant in flow. May Indra, who is beautiful to behold, who like the Aśvin deities goes with ease, and who stands in his chariot, accept our praises and the rest.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
We offer to him (Indra), the most pious (oblations): the worshippers of the gods present to him substantial (adorations): may he, of conspicuous lustre, standing in his car, and light-moving like the Ashwins, be gratified (by our worship).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

अषतरा अस्मै कर्म (*aṣatarā asmai karma*)—अर्षतराणि व्याप्ततराणि वा हवींषि इंद्राय सम्यादयामः (*arṣatarāṇi vyāptatarāṇi vā havīṃṣi iṃdrāya samyādayāmaḥ*). The meaning explained is that the sacrificers, in the sacrifice directed to Indra, for obtaining the satisfaction of Indra,

### Printed p. 264

prepare oblations that are abundant, or that pervade for a long time.

चौत्मानि देवयंतः प्रभरंते (*cautmāni devayaṃtaḥ prabharaṃte*)—दृढानि स्तोत्राणि कर्माणि सामर्थ्यानि वा देवानात्मन इच्छंतो यजमानाः प्रकर्षण सम्यादयंति । (*dṛḍhāni stotrāṇi karmāṇi sāmarthyāni vā devānātmana icchaṃto yajamānāḥ prakarṣaṇa samyādayaṃti |*) **NOTE** The word चौत्न (*cautna*) is stated among the names of strength (Ni. 3-9). They may be acts that make enemies leave (drive them away) from their places. Or, because they call the gods, who have heard the hymns recited by the sacrificers, to the neighbourhood of the sacrificers, Sāyaṇa has explained the word चौत्मानि (*cautmāni*) in two ways, as acts or as hymns. The meaning explained is that the sacrificers, who offer oblations in sacrifices directed to the gods and wish to attain the state of gods, perform acts according to their capacity and please the gods with praises.

The author of the Nirukta has read the word चौत्थ (*cauttha*) among the names of strength such as ओजः, पाजः (*ojaḥ, pājaḥ*) (Ni. 3-9).

शव च्यात्मानि वज्रहस्त शानि नव यत्पुरो नवतिं च सद्यः । (*śava cyātmāni vajrahasta śāni nava yatpuro navatiṃ ca sadyaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-19-5).

In such Ṛks this word is used as a synonym of strength. And:

तमिच्छात्नैरार्यंति तं कृतेभिश्चर्षणीयः । एष इंद्रो वरिवस्कृत् । (*tamicchātnairāryaṃti taṃ kṛtebhiścarṣaṇīyaḥ | eṣa iṃdro varivaskṛt |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-16-6).

In such places it is explained as a praise endowed with strength; and:

स यो न मुहे न मिथू जनो भूत्सुमंतु नामा चुमुरिं धुनिं च । वृणक्षिपुं शंबरं शुष्णमिंद्रः पुरां ज्योतिश्चाय तयथाय नू चित् ॥ (*sa yo na muhe na mithū jano bhūtsumaṃtu nāmā cumuriṃ dhuniṃ ca | vṛṇakṣipuṃ śaṃbaraṃ śuṣṇamiṃdraḥ purāṃ jyotiścāya tayathāya nū cit ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-18-8).

In such Ṛks it is explained as a means of destruction endowed with strength; thus the word is to be explained according to the respective contexts. This word is formed from the root च्यु (*cyu*), which has the meaning "shake, make tremble", and so means "possessing a strength that shakes".

दस्यवर्चाः (*dasyavarcāḥ*)—दर्शनीयतेजाः, शत्रूपक्षयणबलो वा। (*darśanīyatejāḥ, śatrūpakṣayaṇabalo vā|*) The word वर्चस् (*varcas*) is stated among the names of food (Ni. 3-9). Sāyaṇa has explained that it is used in the sense of radiance or strength. The meaning described is that Indra has a radiance fit to be seen by all, and has a strength that destroys enemies.

The word दस्म (*dasma*) has two meanings: one, destroyer of enemies; the other, one of beautiful form. If it is formed from the root हसि दर्शनवंशनयोः (*hasi darśanavaṃśanayoḥ*), it means दर्शनीयः (*darśanīyaḥ*), the delightful one; if formed from the root वसु-उपक्षये (*vasu-upakṣaye*), it means the destroyer of enemies, and

### Printed p. 265
The Kannada text begins in the middle of an explanation of the word दस्म (*dasma*), which is understood in these two senses. Since both kinds of power are found in Indra and the other deities, the commentator shows that either of the two meanings may be said of the word दस्म (*dasma*). For example:

त्वं भगो न आ हि रत्नमिषे परिज्जीव क्षयसि दस्मवर्चाः । (*tvaṃ bhago na ā hi ratnamiṣe parijjīva kṣayasi dasmavarcāḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-13-2)

पूषा सुबंधुर्दिव आ पृथिव्या इळस्पतिर्मघवा दस्मवर्चाः । (*pūṣā subaṃdhurdiva ā pṛthivyā iḷaspatirmaghavā dasmavarcāḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-58-4).

In Ṛks such as these, the commentator shows that the word is understood first as शत्रुपक्षयणबलः (*śatrupakṣayaṇabalaḥ*), one who has the power to destroy enemies, and second, as वर्च इति रूपनाम । दस्मं दर्शनीयं वर्चः यस्य तादृशः (*varca iti rūpanāma | dasmaṃ darśanīyaṃ varcaḥ yasya tādṛśaḥ*), one who has a charming form. The word दस्मवर्चाः (*dasmavarcāḥ*) occurs in many places in the Ṛksaṃhitā as an epithet of Indra and others; so in the present Ṛk too, as an epithet of Indra, it describes his power of destroying enemies and his lovely form.

सुग्मः (*sugmaḥ*)—he is explained as one who is reached easily by the sacrificers; रथेष्ठः (*ratheṣṭhaḥ*)—one who travels in the chariot itself.

नासत्येव (*nāsatyeva*)—नासत्याविव अश्विनाविव तौ हि द्यावापृथिव्यात्मानौ (*nāsatyāviva aśvināviva tau hi dyāvāpṛthivyātmānau*). The authors of the Nirukta, quoting the Ṛk

ते हि यज्ञेषु यज्ञियास ऊमाः सधस्थं विश्वे अभि संति देवाः । ता अध्वर उशतो यक्ष्यग्ने श्रुष्टी भगं नासत्या पुरंधिं ॥ (*te hi yajñeṣu yajñiyāsa ūmāḥ sadhasthaṃ viśve abhi saṃti devāḥ | tā adhvara uśato yakṣyagne śruṣṭī bhagaṃ nāsatyā puraṃdhiṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-39-4)

explain their view on the meaning of the word नासत्य (*nāsatya*) as नासत्यौ सत्यावेव नासत्यावित्यौर्णनाभः, सत्यस्य प्रणेतारावित्याग्रायणः, नासिकाप्रभवौ बभूवतुरिति वा (*nāsatyau satyāveva nāsatyāvityaurṇanābhaḥ, satyasya praṇetārāvityāgrāyaṇaḥ, nāsikāprabhavau babhūvaturiti vā*), that is, the Nāsatyas are the Aśvins. The view of the teacher Aurṇavābha is described: the Aśvin deities are famed as Nāsatyas because they always speak only truth. The teacher Āgrāyaṇa accepts the very etymology सत्यावेव नासत्यौ (*satyāveva nāsatyau*), takes the word सत्य (*satya*) to mean water or sacrifice, and explains that the Aśvin deities are those who carry out the sacrifice. A further explanation is also given, as stated in the Bṛhaddevatā: Sūrya took the form of a horse in order to recognise Saṃjñā-devī, who was in the form of a mare; when he looked at her, the Aśvin deities were born from the region of her nostrils.

Sāyaṇa here gives नासत्याविव (*nāsatyāviva*), "like the Nāsatya Aśvin deities", as the comparison. The Aśvin deities तौ हि द्यावापृथिव्यात्मानौ (*tau hi dyāvāpṛthivyātmānau*), being of the form of heaven and earth,

### Printed p. 266
he explains that they are worthy of being reached with ease and travel by chariot; Indra too, like them, is easy to approach and stands in a chariot. Then he describes the sacrificers' prayer: may he, अस्मृतं स्तोत्रादिकर्म सेवतां (*asmṛtaṃ stotrādikarma sevatāṃ*), take delight in the sacrificial acts, such as hymns of praise, which we the sacrificers perform.

##### Grammar

- ता — तानि इति रूपे शेत्छंदसि बहुलं (पा. सू. ६-१-७०) इति शेर्लोपः ।
- कर्म — डुकृञ् करणे इति धातुः । छंदसिलुज् लज्लिटः (पा. सू ३-४-६) इत्यनेन वर्तमानार्थे लुज् भवति । उत्तमपुरुषबहुवचने मस् प्रत्ययः । नित्यं जातः इति सकारलोपः । मंत्रेघसह्यर—(पा. सू. २-४-८०) इति च्लेर्लुक् । छंदस्युभयथा (पा. सू. ३-४-११७) इति प्रत्ययस्यार्धधातुकसंज्ञायां जित्त्वं न भवति । सार्वधातुकार्धधातुकयोः इति धातोरिको गुणः । रपरत्वं भवति । अतिजंतस्य परत्वान्निघातस्वरो भवति ।
- चौत्नानि — च्युज् च्युतौ इति धातुः । जनिदाच्यु—(उ. पू. ४-५४४) इति त्क्षण् प्रत्ययः । णित्त्वादचोञ्णति इत्युकारस्य वृद्धिः । प्रत्ययस्वरेणांतोदात्तो भवति । **NOTE**
- देवयंतः — देवान् आत्मन इच्छंति इति सुप आत्मनः क्यच् (पा. सू. ३-१-८) इति क्यच् । अस्मात् लटि शत्रादेशः । प्रथमाबहुवचनांतरूपम् ।
- भरंते — भृञ् भरणे इति धातुः । लट् प्रथमपुरुषबहुवचनांतरूपम् । तिजंतनिघातस्वरो भवति ।
- जुषोशत् — जुषी प्रीतिसेवनयोः इति धातुः । लोटि व्यत्ययेन शप् । छांदसत्वेन धातोर्द्वित्वम् । अथवा बहुलं छंदसि इति शपः श्ल्वादेशः । ततो व्यत्ययेन शप् । बहुलं छंदसीति वक्तव्यम् इति वचनान्नाभ्यस्तस्याजिपिति सार्वधातुके (पा. सू. ७-३-८७) इत्यनेन प्राप्तो गुणनिषेधो नात्र भवति । पादादित्वान्निघातस्वरो न भवति । अभ्यस्तानामादिः (पा. सू. ६-१-१८९) इत्याद्युदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- दस्मवर्चाः — दस्म वर्चः यस्य सः दस्मवर्चाः । बहुव्रीहौ प्रकृत्यापूर्वपदम् इति पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरो भवति ।
- सुग्मः — सुष्टु गंतव्यः सुग्म्यः । गम्‌ल्य गतौ इति धातुः । घज्‌र्थे क विधानम् इति वचनात् क प्रत्ययः । कित्त्वाद् गमहनजन—(पा. सू. ६-४-९८) इत्युपधा

### Printed p. 267
- लोपः । सुग्मे भवं सुग्र्यम् । भवे छंडसि (पा सू. ४-४-११०) इति यत् प्रत्ययः । यतोनामः (पा. सू. ६-१-२३) इत्याद्युदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- रथेस्थाः — रथे तिष्ठतीति रथेस्थाः । कृदुत्तरपदप्रकृतिस्वरो भवति । तत्परुषे कृति बहुलम् (पा. सू. ६-३-१९) इति सप्तम्या अलुक् ।

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तमु॑ ष्टु॒हींद्रं॒ यो ह॒ सत्वा॒ यः शूरो॑ म॒घवा॒ यो र॑थे॒ष्ठाः ।  
> प्र॒ती॒चष्टि॒ द्योधी॑या॒न्वष॑ण्वान्वनव॒ु ष॑श्चि॒त्तम॑सो विहं॒ता ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तं । ऊं इति स्तुहि । इंद्रं । यः । ह । सत्वा । यः । शूरः । मघना । यः ।  
> रथेऽस्थाः ।  
> प्रतीचः । चित् । योधीयान् । वृषण्वान् । वववुषः। चित् । तमसः । विहंता ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तमु तमेवेंद्रं स्तुहि। हे होतः स्तुतिं कुरु । किमस्याधिक्यमिति उच्यते ।
> यो ह यः खलु सत्वाशिस्प्रभूतबलः । यद्वा । सत्वा सावकः शत्रूणां । यश्च शूरः
> शौर्योपेतो मघवा बलवानन्नवान्वा । यश्च रथेष्णा रथे वर्तमानः । किंच
> प्रतीचश्चित् प्रत्यभिमुखं योद्धुरपि योधीयान् योद्धृतमः ॥ प्रतिपूर्वादेचतेः क्विनंताल्लुप्तनकाराच्छृसि अच इत्यकारलोपे चाविति दीर्घः । अंचेश्छंदस्यसर्वनामस्थानमिति विभक्तिरुपात्ता ॥ वृषाण्वान् । वृषाणो वर्षितारो वज्रादयः ।
> तष्वान् । वृषा ते वज्र उत ते वृषा रथः । ऋ. २-१६-६ । इति हि निगमः । तथा
> वववुषश्चित्तमस अवरकस्यापि तमसो मेघादेर्निहंता विशेषेण घातकः । यस्मादेवं महानुभावः तस्मात्सु हि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: "Praise that very Indra, O Hotṛ, perform the praise." What is his excellence? यो ह (*yo ha*): he who indeed is सत्वा (*satvā*), of abundant strength; or else सत्वा (*satvā*) is the destroyer of enemies. He who is a hero, endowed with valour; मघवा (*maghavā*), strong or possessed of food; who stands in the chariot. Moreover, he is योधीयान् (*yodhīyān*), the best of fighters, even among those who fight facing him. वृषण्वान् (*vṛṣaṇvān*): the *vṛṣāṇaḥ* are the showerers, the thunderbolt and the like; he possesses them. The Nigama is quoted: वृषा ते वज्र उत ते वृषा रथः । ऋ. २-१६-६ (*vṛṣā te vajra uta te vṛṣā rathaḥ | ṛ. 2-16-6*). Likewise he is the destroyer, the special slayer, even of the all-covering darkness, such as the cloud. Since he is so mighty, therefore praise him.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः ह (*yaḥ ha*)—which Indra indeed
- सत्वा (*satvā*)—one of very abundant strength, or the destroyer of enemies
- यः (*yaḥ*)—who
- शूरः (*śūraḥ*)—a hero, and
- मघवा (*maghavā*)—endowed with strength or food
- यः (*yaḥ*)—

### Printed p. 268
- who
- रथेष्ठाः (*ratheṣṭhāḥ*)—one who is in the chariot
- प्रतीचः चित् (*pratīcaḥ cit*)—even among those who fight facing him
- योधियान् (*yodhiyān*)—the best fighter
- वृषण्वान् (*vṛṣaṇvān*)—the bearer of weapons such as the thunderbolt, and
- यः (*yaḥ*)—who
- वववुषः चित् तमसः (*vavavuṣaḥ cit tamasaḥ*)—the cloud that covers everything
- विहंता (*vihaṃtā*)—the slayer (the piercer)
- तं उ (*taṃ u*)—that same
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- स्तुहि (*stuhi*)—praise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

(O Hotṛ,) praise that Indra who is of great prowess, a hero, rich in wealth, standing in his chariot, who fights facing his enemies, who wields the thunderbolt, and who destroys the cloud and the like, the darkness that covers everything.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Glorify that Indra who is mighty, who is a hero, possessing abundant wealth, standing in his car, a valiant combatant against adversaries, the wielder of the thunderbolt, the disperser of all-enveloping gloom.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

तमुष्टुहि (*tamuṣṭuhi*)—By this sentence the command of the seer who saw the mantra is given here: addressing the priest called Hotṛ, "praise Indra."

यो ह सत्वा (*yo ha satvā*)—अतिप्रभूतबलः, यद्वा सावकः तत्राणां (*atiprabhūtabalaḥ, yadvā sāvakaḥ tatrāṇāṃ*): the meaning is explained as Indra being one of abundant strength, or one who destroys enemies. **NOTE**

मघवा (*maghavā*)—बलवानन्नर्वा वा (*balavānannarvā vā*). The authors of the Nirukta read the word मघ (*magha*) among the names of wealth (Ni. 3-9) and explain: मघमिति धननामधेयं महतेर्दानकर्मणः (*maghamiti dhananāmadheyaṃ mahaterdānakarmaṇaḥ*), that is, the word मघ (*magha*), derived from the root मंह (*maṃha*) which conveys the sense of giving, denotes wealth. Sāyaṇa accepts the use of the word मघ (*magha*) in the senses of strength or food, and explains that Indra is possessed of strength or of food.

Indra is प्रतिचश्चित् योधीर्यान् (*praticaścit yodhīryān*), the best among the heroes who fight facing the enemy who is engaged in battle; and वृषण्वान् (*vṛṣaṇvān*), possessing weapons such as the thunderbolt that assist in pouring rain. For the word वृषण्वान् (*vṛṣaṇvān*) Sāyaṇa gives the meaning वर्षितारः (*varṣitāraḥ*), and explains the sense through the thunderbolt and the like as producers of rain; to convey this same meaning he quotes—

### Printed p. 269
वृषा ते वज्र उत ते वृषा रथो वृषणा हरी वृषभाण्यायुधा । वृष्णो मदस्य वृषभ त्वमीशिष इंद्र सोमस्य वृषभस्य तृप्नुहि ॥ (*vṛṣā te vajra uta te vṛṣā ratho vṛṣaṇā harī vṛṣabhāṇyāyudhā | vṛṣṇo madasya vṛṣabha tvamīśiṣa iṃdra somasya vṛṣabhasya tṛpnuhi ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-16-6)

And he describes Indra's abundant greatness: वववुषश्चित्तमसः विहंता (*vavavuṣaścittamasaḥ vihaṃtā*), the destroyer of the darkness that covers.

##### Grammar

- स्तुहि — ष्टुञ् स्तुत् इति धातुः । लोट् मध्यमपुरुषैकवचनांतरूपम् । तिजंतनिघातस्वरो भवति ।
- सत्वा — षड्ल्‌ विशरणगत्यवसादनेषु इति धातुः । प्रईर सद्योस्तुट् च (उ. सू. ४-५२६) इत्यनेन विधीयमानः क्वनि प्रत्ययो बहुलवचनात् केवलस्यास्य धातोरपि भवति । प्रत्ययस्य पित्त्वादनुदात्तत्वाद् धातुस्वरो अवतिष्ठते ।
- प्रतीचः — प्रति इति उपसर्गः । अंचु गतिपूजनयोः इति धातुः । ऋत्विक् दधृक्—(पा. सू. ३-२-५९) इति क्विन् प्रत्ययः । अनिदितांहलउप—(पा. सू. ६-४-२४) इत्युपधानकारलोपः । शस् परे भसंज्ञत्वादाचः (पा. सू. ६-४-१७८) इति धातोरकारस्य लोपः । लुप्तनकाराकारस्यांचुधातोः परत्वाच्चौ (पा. सू. ६-३-१३८) इति पूर्वस्य दीर्घः । अंचेश्चंदस्युपसंख्यानम् इति वचनाद् विभक्तेरुदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- योधीयान् — योधितृशब्दादतिशयार्थ ईयसुन् प्रत्ययः । तुरिष्टेमेयः सु (पा. सू. ६-४-१५४) इति तृचो लोपः । प्रकृत्यैकाच् इति सूत्रेण प्रकृतिभावात् टिलोपो न भवति । प्रत्ययस्य नित्त्वाद् इत्यादिर्नित्यम् (पा. सू. ६-१-१६९) इत्याद्युदात्तस्वरो भवति ।
- ववप्रुषः — वृञ् आवरणे इति धातुः । चुरादिः । लिटः क्वसुरादेशः । प्रत्ययनिमित्तं द्वित्वादि । छांदसत्वेन वकारोऽधिको भवति । षष्ठ्यां वसोः संप्रसारणम् (पा. सू. ६-४-१३१) इति संप्रसारणं पूर्वरूपम् । शासिवसिघसीनां च (पा. सू. ८-३-६) इति षत्वम् । क्वसुप्रत्ययस्वरो अवतिष्ठते । **NOTE**
- विहंता — हन हिंसागत्योः इति धातुः । कर्तरि तृच् प्रत्ययः । कृदुत्तरपदप्रकृतिस्वरो भवति ।

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र यदि॒त्था म॑हि॒ना नृभ्यो॒ अस्त्यरं॒ रोद॑सी क॒क्ष्ये॒३॒॑ऽनास्मै॑ ।
